denote the triple which uniquely satisfies the continuous Hamiltonian system (
2.19
) with initial random variable
X_{0}
. Suppose that the sequences
\{\rho_{0}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{X_{0}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
are constructed as in Example
2.3
for some
N_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
and sequences of weights
\{\mathcal{A}_{N}\}_{N\geq N_{\dagger}}
, partitions
\{\mathcal{O}_{N}\}_{N\geq N_{\dagger}}
of
\Omega
and simple random variables
\{X_{0}^{N}\}_{N\geq N_{\dagger}}
such that
X_{0}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
for each
N\geq N_{\dagger}
. Then, there exists
k_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}\left(\rho(t),\rho^{k,N}(t)\right)+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(Y-Y^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(X-X^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim N^{-1/d}+\tau_{k}

(4.7)

for all
k\geq k_{\dagger}
and
N\geq N_{\dagger}
. The hidden constant above depends only on
\|X_{0}\|_{L^{2+\delta}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
,
k_{\dagger}
, the time horizon
T
, the uniform convexity constant
c_{0}>0
, and the Lipschitz constants associated with
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
and
D_{x}g
. If in addition
L
and
g
satisfy (
2.27a
) and (
2.27b
), respectively, then

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(u(\cdot,X)-u^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim N^{-1/d}+\tau_{k}

(4.8)

for all
k,N
sufficiently large.

Remark 4.2
.

(On the composite rates of convergence)
With respect to the temporal component of the error in the upper bound in Theorem
4.3
, the
\tau_{k}
term is asymptotically optimal for piecewise constant discretizations since
X
and
Y
are
W^{1,\infty}
-in-time flows. The asymptotic convergence quality of the approximations therefore, in general, boils down to the freedom in the convergence behaviour of the spatial error term
\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}
for
N
large. For instance, the convergence error bound achieved by the semi-discrete optimal transport approximations for
X_{0}
given in Example
2.2
is optimal among initial measures
\rho_{0}
in
\mathcal{P}_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
q>2
, except for some critical cases
[
82
]
. In addition, by Example
2.3
approximations of
X_{0}
that are obtained by piecewise constant optimal vector quantization converge strongly in the
L^{2}
-norm, with the error asymptotically residing within the closed interval
[N^{-\frac{1}{\mathcal{(}1-\epsilon){D}_{L}(\rho_{0})}},N^{-\frac{1}{\mathcal{D}_{U}(\rho_{0})+\epsilon\mathcal{D}_{L}(\rho_{0})}}]
, given
0<\epsilon<1
, which depends on the lower and upper quantization dimensions of
\rho_{0}.

Uniqueness for a class of discrete Hamiltonian systems

The main result we establish in this section is a continuous dependence bound for a class of generalized discrete Hamiltonian systems, which includes the numerical scheme (
3.7
) as a particular case. This class of systems is given as follows. Recall that the sequences
\{\rho_{0}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{X_{0}^{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, and hence the sequences
\{\mathcal{A}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\mathcal{O}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, are assumed given and fixed. Let
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\iota_{k}\coloneqq\{t_{1},t_{2},\cdots,t_{M_{k}-1}\}
. For each
\lambda\in[0,1]
,
F,G\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
and
X_{0}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
, we define the following problem:
find
\mathscr{X}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
,
\mathscr{Y}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
and
\varrho^{k,N}\in\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,N})
such that

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\mathscr{X}^{k,N}(t)&=\lambda D_{p}H\left(\mathscr{X}^{k,N}(t),\mathscr{Y}^{k,N}(t),\varrho^{k,N}(t)\right)+F(t),&&t\in(0,T)\backslash\iota_{k},\\
\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\mathscr{Y}^{k,N}(t)&=-\lambda D_{x}H\left(\mathscr{X}^{k,N}(t),\mathscr{Y}^{k,N}(t),\varrho^{k,N}(t)\right)+G(t),&&t\in(0,T)\backslash\iota_{k},\\
\varrho^{k,N}(t)&=(\mathscr{X}^{k,N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P},&&t\in[0,T],\\
\mathscr{X}^{k,N}(0)&=\lambda X_{0}^{N},\quad\mathscr{Y}^{k,N}(T)=-\lambda D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}^{k,N}(T),\varrho^{k,N}(T))&&\text{ }\end{aligned}\right.

(5.1)

where
\iota_{k}\coloneqq\{t_{m}:q=1,2,\cdots,M_{k}-1\}
.
Systems of this form enjoy the following continuous dependence property, which is the main result of this section.

Theorem 5.1

(Continuous dependence for generalized discrete Hamiltonian system)
.

Let the Hamiltonian
H
satisfy the regularity assumptions (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
2.5
), (
2.6
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.8
). Let the terminal cost
g
satisfy the regularity assumptions (
2.9
), (
2.10
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.12
). Let
N,M,k\in\mathbb{N}
and

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\in(0,1],\quad F_{1},G_{1}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\quad X_{0,1}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(5.2a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}\in[0,1],\quad F_{2},G_{2}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})),\quad X_{0,2}^{M}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})

(5.2b)

be given, and suppose that the triples

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,N})

(5.3a)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,M})

(5.3b)

each satisfy the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) with data (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
), respectively. There exists
k_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for each
k\geq k_{\dagger}
, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}&\left(\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(t),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(t)\right)+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\lVert(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(t)\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})(t)\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)\\
&\lesssim\lVert X_{0,1}^{N}-\lambda_{2}\lambda_{1}^{-1}X_{0,2}^{M}\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lambda_{1}^{-1}\left(\|F_{1}-F_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}+\|G_{1}-G_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\right)\\
&+\lvert\lambda_{2}\lambda_{1}^{-1}-1\rvert\left(\lVert D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\left\|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left\|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\right)\end{split}

(5.4)

The number
k_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
and the hidden constant in (
5.4
) depend only on the uniform convexity constant
c_{0}
,
T
, and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H,D_{x}H
and
D_{x}g
.

We therefore obtain Theorem
4.2
as a particular corollary of Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

We deduce the desired bound (
4.1
) by setting
N=M\in\mathbb{N}
,
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=1
,
F_{1}=F_{2}=G_{1}=G_{2}=0
in
\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
in (
5.4
) of Theorem . If in addition we set
X_{0,1}^{N}\coloneqq X_{0,2}^{N}
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
, the uniqueness of solutions to the numerical scheme (
3.7
) is immediate.
∎

Remark 5.1

(Uniqueness of solutions for generalized discrete Hamiltonian system)
.

Theorem
5.1
implies that the generalized discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) can admit at most one solution for each fixed
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\lambda\in(0,1]
,
F,G\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
and
X_{0}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
, where
k\geq k_{\dagger}
. A direct calculation shows that uniqueness for the system also extends to the case when
\lambda=0
, with the explicit solution being linear in the terms
F
and
G
. We note, in particular, that in the special case when
F=G=0
in
\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
,
\lambda=0
and any
X_{0}^{N}
is fixed, the triple
(\mathscr{X}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}^{k,N},\varrho^{k,N})=(0,0,\delta_{0})
uniquely satisfies the system (
5.1
).

The proof of Theorem
5.1
that we present can be considered an extension of
[
69
, Theorem 4.5]
to discrete Hamiltonian systems of the form (
5.1
). In particular, the argument we employ shows how displacement monotonicity conditions on
H
and
g
, together with strong convexity of the Hamiltonian, ensure stability of solutions to discrete Hamiltonian systems under continuous affine perturbations.

The first step in the proof of Theorem
5.1
is the following preliminary stability result.

Lemma 5.2

(Displacement monotonicity stability)
.

Let the Hamiltonian
H
satisfy the regularity assumption (
2.2
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.8
). Let the terminal cost
g
satisfy the regularity assumption (
2.9
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.12
). Let
N,M,k\in\mathbb{N}
and

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\in(0,1],\quad F_{1},G_{1}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\quad X_{0,1}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(5.5a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}\in[0,1],\quad F_{2},G_{2}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})),\quad X_{0,2}^{M}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})

(5.5b)

be given, and suppose that the triples

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,N}),

(5.6a)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,M})

(5.6b)

each satisfy the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) with data (
5.5a
) and (
5.5b
), respectively.
For all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
, we have

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\lambda_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad-(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})\cdot\left(D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(T)\right]\\
&\qquad-\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(0)\cdot(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})(0)\right]-\mathfrak{I}(0,T),\end{split}

(5.7)

where the map
\mathfrak{I}:[0,T]^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined via

\begin{split}\mathfrak{I}(a,b)\coloneqq&\int_{a}^{b}(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\cdot(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})\cdot D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad+\int_{a}^{b}\mathbb{E}\left[(F_{1}-F_{2})\cdot(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})+(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})\cdot(G_{1}-G_{2})\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\forall a,b\in[0,T].\end{split}

(5.8)

Remark 5.2
.

Notice that the upper bound in (
5.7
) is uniform in the temporal node index
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. As we now show, this result is a basic stability property of the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) that is due solely to the displacement monotonicity of
H
and
g
.

The proofs of the upcoming lemmata will employ the following discrete integration-by-parts formulae that are satisfied by the time reconstruction operators
\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}
and
\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}
in (
3.3
), (
3.4
) in the case when
A=L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Given
n,m\in\{0,1,\cdots,{M_{k}}\}
we have

\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{s}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}V_{1}(s)\cdot W_{1}(s)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[V_{1}(s)\cdot\partial_{s}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}W_{1}(s)\right]{\mathrm{d}s}=\mathbb{E}[V_{1}(t_{n})\cdot W_{1}(t_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[V_{1}(t_{m})\cdot W_{1}(t_{m})],

(5.9)

\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{s}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}V_{1}(s)\cdot V_{2}(s)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[V_{1}(s)\cdot\partial_{s}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}V_{2}(s)\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
=\mathbb{E}[V_{1}(t_{n})\cdot V_{2}(t_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[V_{1}(t_{m})\cdot V_{2}(t_{m})]+\sum_{j=m+1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\llbracket V_{1}\rrbracket_{j-1}\cdot\llbracket V_{2}\rrbracket_{j-1}\right],

(5.10)

\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{s}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}W_{1}(s)\cdot W_{2}(s)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[W_{1}(s)\cdot\partial_{s}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}W_{2}(s)\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
=\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(t_{n})\cdot W_{2}(t_{n})]-\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(t_{m})\cdot W_{2}(t_{m})]-\sum_{j=m+1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\llbracket W_{1}\rrbracket_{j}\cdot\llbracket W_{2}\rrbracket_{j}\right],

(5.11)

for all
V_{1},V_{2}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
W_{1},W_{2}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
.

For completeness, the proof of this lemma is included in Appendix
A
.

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

Let
k,N,M\in\mathbb{N}
be given. Suppose that the triples

(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\ {\rm and}\ (\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})

in (
5.6a
), (
5.6b
) each satisfy the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) with data (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
), respectively. Let
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. For each
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
the systems satisfied by
(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N}),(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})
respectively imply

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad=\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\lambda_{1}D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-\lambda_{2}D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[(F_{1}-F_{2})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(5.12)

and

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=-\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\lambda_{1}D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-\lambda_{2}D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[(G_{1}-G_{2})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}.\end{split}

(5.13)

By summing these two equations we get

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}+\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\lambda_{1}D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-\lambda_{2}D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad-\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\left(\lambda_{1}D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-\lambda_{2}D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[(F_{1}-F_{2})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}+\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot(G_{1}-G_{2})\right]\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

(5.14)

which is the same as

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}+\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\lambda_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad-(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})\cdot\left(D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad+\mathfrak{I}(t_{n},T)\end{split}

(5.15)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
where
\mathfrak{I}
is defined as in (
5.8
).
Since
H
satisfies the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.8
) and
\lambda_{1}>0
we deduce from (
5.15
) that

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}+\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\geq\mathfrak{I}(t_{n},T)\end{split}

(5.16)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
.

Furthermore, we infer from displacement monotonicity of
g
via (
2.12
) and non-negativity of
\lambda_{1}\in(0,1]
that

\lambda_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[(D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T),\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T))-D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T)))\cdot\left(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T)-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T)\right)\right]\geq 0

(5.17)

which is equivalent to

(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T)-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T))\right]\\
\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(-\lambda_{1}D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T),\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T))+\lambda_{2}D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\right)\cdot\left(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T)-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T)\right)\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\right]

(5.18)

since
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)=\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T)-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T)
and we have the terminal time expression

\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(T)=\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}(T)-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}(T)=-\lambda_{1}D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T),\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T))+\lambda_{2}D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T)).

This bound and the integration-by-parts formula (
5.9
) then imply that

\begin{split}&(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T)-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T))\right]\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\right]+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}+\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(5.19)

for
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. We then deduce from this and (
5.16
) that

\begin{split}&(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\right]\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\right]+\mathfrak{I}(t_{n},T)\end{split}

(5.20)

and thus

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\right]\leq(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\right]-\mathfrak{I}(t_{n},T)\end{split}

(5.21)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
.

We also have from the systems satisfied by
(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N}),(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})
respectively that

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}+\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\lambda_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad-\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\left(D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad+\mathfrak{I}(0,t_{n})\end{split}

(5.22)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Consequently, (
5.21
) and the integration-by-parts formula (
5.9
) give

\begin{split}&(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\right]-\mathfrak{I}(t_{n},T)\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\right]+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}+\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(5.23)

We then deduce from this and (
5.22
) that

\begin{split}&(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\right]-\mathfrak{I}(t_{n},T)\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\right]\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\lambda_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad-\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\left(D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad+\mathfrak{I}(0,t_{n}).\end{split}

(5.24)

We then conclude the required bound after rearranging and noting that
\mathfrak{I}(0,t_{n})+\mathfrak{I}(t_{n},T)=\mathfrak{I}(0,T)
.
∎

The second step in proving Theorem
5.1
showcases a stronger stability property obtained under the additional assumption on strong convexity of
H
and Lipschitz continuity of both
D_{p}H
and
D_{x}H
.

Lemma 5.4

(Strong convexity stability)
.

Let the Hamiltonian
H
satisfy the regularity assumptions (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
2.5
), (
2.6
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.8
). Let the terminal cost
g
satisfy the regularity assumption (
2.9
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.12
). Let
N,M,k\in\mathbb{N}
and

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\in(0,1],\quad F_{1},G_{1}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\quad X_{0,1}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(5.25a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}\in[0,1],\quad F_{2},G_{2}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})),\quad X_{0,2}^{M}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})

(5.25b)

be given, and suppose that the triples

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,N}),

(5.26a)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,M})

(5.26b)

each satisfy the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) with data (
5.25a
) and (
5.25b
), respectively. Then, for all
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
and all
0<\epsilon<1
, there holds

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(5.27)

where the hidden constant in (
5.27
) depends only on
c_{0}
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H
and
D_{x}H
, and where we define the quantities

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{E}_{1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\lambda_{1}^{2}}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}\left[|(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(0)|^{2}+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\lvert D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert F_{1}-F_{2}\rvert^{2}+\lvert G_{1}-G_{2}\rvert^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
&\quad\qquad+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\rvert^{2}+\lvert D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\rvert^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\Bigr),\end{split}

(5.28)

\mathfrak{E}_{2}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[|(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})(0)|^{2}+|(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(T)|^{2}\right]\\
+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}|^{2}+|\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}.

(5.29)

Proof.

We prove the result in the case when the sum
{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)
of the Lipschitz constants for
D_{p}H
and
D_{x}H
is positive. The proof in the the case when one or both of these constants are zero follows similarly by a more direct argument.

Let
k,N,M\in\mathbb{N}
be given. We suppose that the triples
(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})

(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})
with (
5.26a
), (
5.26b
) each satisfy the discrete system (
5.1
) with data (
5.5a
) and (
5.5b
), respectively. We then let
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. We begin the proof by employing the strong convexity of
H
w.r.t.
p
to obtain an estimate for the generalized momentum difference
W_{N,M}^{k}=\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}
. This this end, we rearrange the bound (
5.7
) from Lemma
5.2
and introduce the term
D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\cdot(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})
on both sides of the bound to get

\begin{split}&\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\right)\cdot(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\right]-\mathfrak{I}(0,T)\\
&\quad\quad+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\quad\quad+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\left(D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(5.30)

for all
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Using the strong convexity of
H
w.r.t.
p
via (
2.6
), we get

\begin{split}&\lambda_{1}c_{0}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|\right]\\
&\quad+|\mathfrak{I}(0,T)|+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\right||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\quad+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\left|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right|\right]\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(5.31)

for all
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Note that the uniform Lipschitz continuity of both
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
with respect to the Euclidean norm in the vector arguments and
\mathcal{W}_{1}
-metric in the measure argument via (
2.3
), together with the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality applied to the integral in the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{1}
, implies the uniform Lipschitz continuity of both
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
with respect to the Euclidean norm in the vector arguments and
\mathcal{W}_{2}
-metric in the measure argument. This leads to

\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\right||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\lambda_{1}{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s

(5.32)

\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\left|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\lambda_{1}{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\left(|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|+|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s.

(5.33)

With
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
fixed, we now aim to extract the integral
\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}s
from the upper bounds in (
5.32
) and (
5.33
) by applying Young’s inequality repeatedly.
Indeed, by introducing a parameter
\gamma\in(0,1)
, we apply Young’s inequality to the integrand in the upper bound of (
5.32
) to get

\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\right||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{\lambda_{1}{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma}|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)+\frac{\gamma}{2}|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s,

(5.34)

and likewise Young’s inequality applied to the integrand in the upper bound of (
5.33
) gives

\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\left|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\lambda_{1}{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{2}|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right]\mathrm{d}s

(5.35)

Adding these bounds (
5.34
), (
5.35
) together gives

\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\right||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\left|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{\lambda_{1}}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{3{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{2}+\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{2\gamma}\right)|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]\\
+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\left[\left(\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)}{2\gamma}+\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{2}\right)\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\left({\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\right)\gamma}{2}\right)|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s,

(5.36)

To obtain coefficients in the upper bound that are more compact, let us assume further that
0<\gamma<1/3
, to have

\frac{3{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{2}+\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{2\gamma}\leq\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+2{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{2\gamma}\leq\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{\gamma}

(5.37)

\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)}{2\gamma}+\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{2}\leq\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{\gamma}

(5.38)

and clearly
\left({\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\right)\gamma/2\leq\left({\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\right)\gamma
.
We thus obtain

\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\right||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
+\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\left|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{\lambda_{1}}\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\lambda_{1}\left({\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\right)\gamma\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s,

(5.39)

With this simplified upper bound, we return to the bound (
5.31
) and rearrange to get

\begin{split}&\lambda_{1}\left(c_{0}-\left({\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\right)\gamma\right)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|\right]+|\mathfrak{I}(0,T)|\\
&\quad+{\lambda_{1}}\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(5.40)

for all
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
and
0<\gamma<1/3
. By fixing
0<\gamma<\min\left\{\frac{1}{3},\frac{c_{0}}{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}\right\}
we obtain the bound

\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq C_{\gamma}\left(|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|\right]\right.\\
\left.+|\mathfrak{I}(0,T)|+{\lambda_{1}}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right)

(5.41)

for all
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
, with the positive constant

C_{\gamma}\coloneqq\max\left\{\frac{1}{c_{0}-({\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H))\gamma},\frac{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}{\left(c_{0}-({\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H))\gamma\right)\gamma}\right\}

(5.42)

with fixed constant
0<\gamma<\min\left\{\frac{1}{3},\frac{c_{0}}{{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)}\right\}
.

Next, we aim to bound the term

|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|\right]+|\mathfrak{I}(0,T)|.

By Young’s inequality with parameter
0<\lambda_{1}\epsilon<1
where
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, have

|\mathfrak{I}(0,T)|\leq|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right.\\
\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad+|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}||D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|+|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}||G_{1}-G_{2}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\frac{(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}}{2\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}+|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
+\frac{1}{2\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}|^{2}+|G_{1}-G_{2}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s+{\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}+|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s

(5.43)

and

|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|\right]\\
\leq\frac{(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}}{2\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))|^{2}\right]\\
+\frac{\lambda_{1}\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{2\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|^{2}\right]+\frac{\lambda_{1}\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|^{2}\right].

(5.44)

We then obtain

|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|\right]+|\mathfrak{I}(0,T)|\\
\leq\mathscr{A}_{1}+\mathscr{A}_{2}+\mathscr{A}_{3}

(5.45)

where

\mathscr{A}_{1}\coloneqq\frac{(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}}{2\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}+|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\right.\\
\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))|^{2}\right]\right),

(5.46)

\mathscr{A}_{2}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}|^{2}+|G_{1}-G_{2}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|^{2}\right]\right),

(5.47)

and

\mathscr{A}_{3}\coloneqq{\lambda_{1}\epsilon}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}+|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|^{2}\right]\right).

(5.48)

In view of the definition of
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
in (
5.28
) and
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
in (
5.29
), we see that
\mathscr{A}_{1}+\mathscr{A}_{2}=\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}
and
\mathscr{A}_{3}=\lambda_{1}\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}
. Therefore,

|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|\right]+|\mathfrak{I}(0,T)|\\
\leq\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\lambda_{1}\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}.

(5.49)

In all, combining (
5.41
) and (
5.49
), we get

\begin{split}&\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C_{\gamma}}{2\epsilon}\lambda_{1}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+{C_{\gamma}}\epsilon\lambda_{1}\mathfrak{E}_{2}+C_{\gamma}\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right){\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(5.50)

for all
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
and all constant
0<\epsilon<1
, where the quantities
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
are defined as in (
5.28
) and (
5.29
), respectively, and
C_{\gamma}
is the constant defined in (
5.42
) which depends only on the Lipschitz constants
{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)
and
{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)
, and the strong convexity constant
c_{0}>0
. Since
\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(t)
and
\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(t)
are laws for
\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(t)
and
\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
, respectively, from the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
we have the bound

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(t),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(t))\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t)\rvert^{2}\right]\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

(5.51)

It then follows that

\begin{split}&\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C_{\gamma}}{2\epsilon}\lambda_{1}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+{C_{\gamma}}\epsilon\lambda_{1}\mathfrak{E}_{2}+2C_{\gamma}\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(5.52)

for all
n\in\{0,1,\cdots,M_{k}\}
and all constant
0<\epsilon<1
. Divide this bound by
\lambda_{1}\in(0,1]
to obtain (
5.27
), as desired.
∎

If in addition one has that
D_{x}g
is Lipschitz continuous, we show that the preceding lemma and a discrete Grönwall’s inequality allow us to deduce an
L^{\infty}
-in-time stability bound for the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
).

Lemma 5.5

(Uniform
L^{\infty}
-stability in time as
k\to\infty
)
.

Let the Hamiltonian
H
satisfy the regularity assumptions (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
2.5
), (
2.6
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.8
). Let the terminal cost
g
satisfy the regularity assumptions (
2.9
), (
2.10
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.12
). Let
N,M,k\in\mathbb{N}
and

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\in(0,1],\quad F_{1},G_{1}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\quad X_{0,1}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(5.53a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}\in[0,1],\quad F_{2},G_{2}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})),\quad X_{0,2}^{M}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M})

(5.53b)

be given, and suppose that the triples

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,N}),

(5.54a)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{M},\mathcal{O}_{M}))\times\mathbb{P}_{k}([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{d,M})

(5.54b)

each satisfy the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) with data (
5.53a
) and (
5.53b
), respectively. There exists
k_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(t)\rvert^{2}\right]+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})(t)\rvert^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}

(5.55)

for each
k\geq k_{\dagger}
and all
0<\epsilon<1
, where
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
are defined as in (
5.28
) and (
5.29
), respectively. The hidden constant in (
5.55
) depends only on
k_{\dagger}
,
T
,
c_{0}
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
, and
D_{x}g
.

The proof of this result will rely on the following discrete version of the Grönwall Lemma, c.f.
[
56
, Lemma 2]
,
[
39
, Proposition 4.1]
.

Lemma 5.6

(Discrete Grönwall Lemma)
.

Let
T>0
be constant, and let
\tau_{k}>0
,
M_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfy
T=\tau_{k}M_{k}
and
M_{k+1}\geq M_{k}
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
M_{k}\to\infty
as
k\to\infty
. Let constants
b_{x},b_{y},c_{x},c_{y}\geq 0
and
k_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
be given. For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\{x_{n}\}_{n=1}^{M_{k}}
be a sequence of non-negative numbers which satisfy

x_{n}\leq b_{x}+c_{x}\tau_{k}\sum_{m=1}^{n}x_{m},

(5.56)

for
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Then, for each
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{x}
,

\max_{0\leq n\leq M_{k}}x_{n}\leq b_{x}\exp\left(\frac{c_{x}T}{1-c_{x}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}}\right),

(5.57)

where
k_{\dagger}^{x}
is the smallest positive integer such that
1-c_{x}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}>0
.

For each
k\geq k_{*}
, let
\{y_{n}\}_{n=1}^{M_{k}}
be a sequence of non-negative numbers which satisfy

y_{n}\leq b_{y}+c_{y}\tau_{k}\sum_{m=n+1}^{M_{k}}y_{m-1}

(5.58)

for
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Then, for each
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{y}
,

\max_{0\leq n\leq M_{k}}y_{n}\leq b_{y}\exp\left(\frac{c_{y}T}{1-c_{y}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{y}}}\right),

(5.59)

where
k_{\dagger}^{y}
is the smallest positive integer such that
1-c_{x}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{y}}>0
and
k_{\dagger}^{y}\geq k_{*}
.

For completeness, the proof of this lemma is provided in Appendix
A
.

Proof of Lemma
5.5
.

Let
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
and
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
. The integration-by-parts formula (
5.10
) then implies that

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\rvert^{2}\right]+2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\llbracket\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rrbracket_{j-1}\right|^{2}\right]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\rvert^{2}\right]+2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s

(5.60)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. The systems satisfied by
(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N}),(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})
respectively then imply that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad+2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\lambda_{1}D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-\lambda_{2}D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[(F_{1}-F_{2})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\quad+2\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
&\qquad+2(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
&\qquad\qquad+2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[(F_{1}-F_{2})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(5.61)

for
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
.
Lipschitz continuity of
D_{p}H
via (
2.3
) implies that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\quad+2{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
&\quad+2|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(5.62)

where we used the fact that
0<\lambda_{1}\leq 1
. We apply Young’s inequality, without parameter, to each of the latter three integral terms of the upper bound to get

2{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
\leq 2{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)\left(\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[2\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right]\mathrm{d}s\right),

(5.63)

2|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
\leq(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s},

(5.64)

2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}||\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\leq\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}

(5.65)

for each
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Adding these bounds, we then obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
\leq(4{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)+2)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M}){\mathrm{d}s}\\
+{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\rvert^{2}\right]\\
+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}

(5.66)

for each
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Since
0<\lambda_{1}\leq 1
and
t_{n}\in(0,T)
for
for each
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
, we have the bound

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(0)\rvert^{2}\right]+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|F_{1}-F_{2}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\leq\mathfrak{E}_{1}

(5.67)

after recalling the definition of
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
in (
5.28
). We therefore deduce that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right]{\mathrm{d}s},\end{split}

(5.68)

for
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
, where the hidden constant depends only on
{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)
and where
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
is defined in (
5.28
). Using (
5.51
), together with (
5.27
) from Lemma
5.4
, gives for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
and
0<\epsilon<1

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\lesssim\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\end{split}

(5.69)

where the hidden constant in (
5.69
) depends on only
c_{0}
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H,D_{x}H
.

Recall that
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}
is piecewise constant in time with values in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and is left-continuous. As such, the bound (
5.69
) can be written equivalently as a discrete summation inequality of the form

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\leq C_{X}\left(\tau_{k}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{m})\rvert^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\right)

(5.70)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
where
C_{X}
is the hidden constant in (
5.69
). Take the smallest positive integer
k_{\dagger}^{x}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
1-C_{X}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}>0
. Note then that such
k_{\dagger}^{x}
depends only
c_{0}
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H,D_{x}H
. We can therefore apply the forward-in-time discrete Gronwall inequality (
5.57
) from Lemma
5.6
to deduce that

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\leq C_{X}\exp\left(\frac{C_{X}T}{1-C_{X}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\right)

(5.71)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
,
0<\epsilon<1
and
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{x}
, where the constant
C_{X}\exp\left(\frac{C_{X}T}{1-C_{X}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}}\right)
above depends only on
k_{\dagger}^{x}
,
T
,
c_{0}
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H,D_{x}H
.
Since
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}
is left-continuous in
[0,T]
, this bound holds for all
t\in[0,T]
, i.e.

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t)\rvert^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}

(5.72)

for all
0<\epsilon<1
and all
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{x}
. The hidden constant in the above bound depends on only
k_{\dagger}^{x}
,
c_{0}
,
T
, and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H,D_{x}H
.

To prove the analogous bound for the
Y
-component of the stability bound we follow the same approach. Indeed, we infer from the integration by parts formula (
5.11
) and the systems satisfied by

(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N}),(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M}),

respectively, that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\rvert^{2}\right]-2\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s-\sum_{m=n+1}^{M_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\llbracket\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rrbracket_{m}|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\rvert^{2}\right]-2\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\lambda_{1}D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T),\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T))-\lambda_{2}D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad+2\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\lambda_{1}D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-\lambda_{2}D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right)\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad-2\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[(G_{1}-G_{2})\cdot\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(5.73)

which implies

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\leq 2\lambda_{1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}(T),\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T))-D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\quad+2(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rvert^{2}\right]\\
&\quad+2\lambda_{1}\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})-D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\quad+2|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s+2\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{1}-G_{2}||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(5.74)

for
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
.

Lipschitz continuity of
D_{x}g
and
D_{x}H
via (
2.10
),(
2.3
) and the assumption
0<\lambda_{1}\leq 1
imply that

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
\leq 4{\rm Lip}(D_{x}g)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\rvert^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\right]+2(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rvert^{2}\right]\\
\quad+2{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\quad+2|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s+2\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{1}-G_{2}||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]{\mathrm{d}s}.

(5.75)

Using Young’s inequality, without parameter, we obtain that

2{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq 2{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\left(\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[2\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right]\mathrm{d}s\right),

(5.76)

2|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s},

(5.77)

2\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{1}-G_{2}||\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{1}-G_{2}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s},

(5.78)

for each
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Adding these bounds we obtain from (
5.75
)

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\\
\leq 4{\rm Lip}(D_{x}g)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\rvert^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\right]+(4{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)+2)\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M}){\mathrm{d}s}+{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
+2(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rvert^{2}\right]+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{1}-G_{2}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}

(5.79)

Since
0<\lambda_{1}\leq 1
and
t_{n}\in(0,T)
for each
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
, we have that

2(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert D_{x}g(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}(T),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(T))\rvert^{2}\right]+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|G_{1}-G_{2}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\leq 2\mathfrak{E}_{1}

(5.80)

where we recall that
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
is defined in (
5.28
). We have therefore shown that

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\rvert^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(T),\rho_{2}(T))\right]\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\mathfrak{E}_{1}

(5.81)

whence (
5.51
) implies

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)\rvert^{2}\right]+\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(5.82)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
, where the hidden constant depends only on the Lipschitz constants of
D_{x}H
and
D_{x}g
. The uniform bound (
5.72
) for the difference in the solutions to the discrete state equations then gives

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\leq C_{Y}\int_{t_{n}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}+\tilde{C}_{X}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\right)\end{split}

(5.83)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
,
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{x}
, and all
0<\epsilon<1
, where the constant
C_{Y}
depends only on the Lipschitz constants of
D_{x}H
and
D_{x}g
, while the constant
\tilde{C}_{X}
takes the form
\tilde{C}_{X}\coloneqq C_{Y}(1+T)C_{X}\exp\left(\frac{C_{X}T}{1-C_{X}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}}\right)
and depends only on
k_{\dagger}^{x},c_{0},T
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H,D_{x}g
.
Recalling that
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}
is piecewise constant in time with values in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and is right-continuous, the above bound (
5.83
) is equivalent to

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\leq C_{Y}\tau_{k}\sum_{m=n+1}^{M_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{m-1})\rvert^{2}\right]+\tilde{C}_{X}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\right)

(5.84)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
and
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{x}
. Taking
k_{\dagger}^{y}
to be the smallest positive integer such that
1-C_{Y}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{y}}>0
and
k_{\dagger}^{y}\geq k_{\dagger}^{x}
, we take
k_{*}\coloneqq k_{\dagger}^{x}
and apply the backward-in-time discrete Gronwall inequality (
5.59
) from Lemma
5.6
to deduce that

\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t_{n})\rvert^{2}\right]\leq\tilde{C}_{X}\exp\left(\frac{C_{Y}T}{1-C_{Y}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{y}}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\right)

(5.85)

for all
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
,
0<\epsilon<1
, and
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{y}
. Note that
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}
is right-continuous in
[0,T]
since
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
. Consequently, this bound holds for all
t\in[0,T]
, thereby implying

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t)\rvert^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}

(5.86)

for all
0<\epsilon<1
and
k\geq k_{\dagger}\coloneqq k_{\dagger}^{y}
. The hidden constant in the above bound takes the form
C_{Y}(1+T)C_{X}\exp\left(\frac{C_{X}T}{1-C_{X}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}}\right)\exp\left(\frac{C_{Y}T}{1-C_{Y}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{y}}}\right)
depends on only
k_{\dagger}^{x},c_{0},T
and the Lipschitz constants of the functions
D_{p}H,D_{x}H,D_{x}g
. The proof is completed upon summing (
5.72
) and (
5.86
) for each
k\geq k_{\dagger}
, noting then that the hidden constant in the resultant bound depends on only
c_{0},T
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H,D_{x}H,D_{x}g
since
k_{\dagger}^{x}
depends only on
c_{0}
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H,D_{x}H
and
D_{x}g
.
∎

We now complete the proof of the main result of this section, namely Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Assume that the Hamiltonian
H
satisfies the regularity assumptions (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
2.5
), (
2.6
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.8
). Further assume that the terminal cost
g
satisfies the regularity assumptions (
2.9
), (
2.10
) and the displacement monotonicity condition (
2.12
). Let
k,N,M\in\mathbb{N}
be given. Suppose that the triples
(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})
,
(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})
in (
5.6a
), (
5.6b
) each satisfy the discrete Hamiltonian system (
5.1
) with data (
5.2a
) and (
5.2b
), respectively.
Let
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
and
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\coloneqq\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
. By Lemma
5.5
, there exists
k_{\dagger}
such that, for each
k\geq k_{\dagger}
, we have the bound (
5.55
). From this bound we immediately deduce that, for
k\geq k_{\dagger}
,

\mathfrak{E}_{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[|(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})(0)|^{2}+|(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(T)|^{2}\right]\\
+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}|^{2}+|\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\\
\leq(2+T)\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M})(t)\rvert^{2}\right]+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert(\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}-\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M})(t)\rvert^{2}\right]\right)\\
\lesssim(2+T)\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\right),

(5.87)

since
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k},\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. We have hence shown that

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{E}_{2}\leq C_{*}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathfrak{E}_{1}+\epsilon\mathfrak{E}_{2}\right)\end{split}

(5.88)

for all
0<\epsilon<1
and
k\geq k_{\dagger}
, where the constant
C_{*}
depends on only
c_{0},T
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
, and
D_{x}g
. In fact, from the proof of the previous Lemma
5.5
, this constant can be taken to be

C_{*}=2(T+2)(T+1)\max\{C_{X},C_{Y}\}\exp\left(\left(\frac{C_{X}}{1-C_{X}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{x}}}+\frac{C_{Y}}{1-C_{Y}\tau_{k_{\dagger}^{y}}}\right)T\right)

(5.89)

Therefore, taking
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2}C_{*}>0
, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(0)|^{2}+|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(T)|^{2}\right]+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}\rvert^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s=\mathfrak{E}_{2}\leq 4C_{*}^{2}\mathfrak{E}_{1}

(5.90)

for all
k\geq k_{\dagger}
. It then follows from (
5.55
) and (
5.51
) that, for
k\geq k_{\dagger}
, there holds

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\varrho_{1}^{k,N}(t),\varrho_{2}^{k,M}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,M}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,M}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\mathfrak{E}_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

(5.91)

where
\mathfrak{E}_{1}
is as defined in (
5.28
). The hidden constant here takes the form
3C_{*}
, where
C_{*}
is defined in (
5.89
), which depends on only
c_{0}
,
T
, and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H,
and
D_{x}g
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Existence of numerical approximations

In this section we establish Theorem
4.1
on the existence of solutions to the numerical scheme (
3.7
). The strategy we employ will be based on the Leray–Schauder–Schaefer fixed point theorem. For completeness, we formulate here the version of this theorem that is given in
[
45
, Theorem 11.3]
.

Theorem 6.1

(Leray–Schauder–Schaefer fixed point theorem)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a continuous, compact mapping of a Banach space
\mathfrak{B}
into itself, and suppose there exists a constant
M
such that
\|x\|_{\mathfrak{B}}<M
for all
x\in\mathfrak{B}
and
\beta\in[0,1]
satisfying
x=\beta\mathcal{T}x
. Then
\mathcal{T}
has a fixed point.

Remark 6.1
.

In the analysis of systems of partial differential equations associated to MFGs, such as (
1.1
), a typical approach to establishing the existence of solutions is an application of Schauder’s fixed point theorem, in the case where the system has differentiable Hamiltonians, e.g.
[
4
]
, or its set-valued generalization in Kakutani’s fixed point theorem, when the Hamiltonian of the system is nondifferentiable, see e.g.
[
37
,
72
,
73
]
. In either of those settings, formulating a fixed-point solution map with bounded images is often made feasible under some sufficient conditions which ensure uniform boundedness of solutions to the KFP equation of the system.

However, in our setting where we study discrete-in-time Hamiltonian ODE systems under some general hypotheses for the model data, no such boundedness property is ensured for solutions to either of the ODE subproblems if one unknown of the system is fixed, unless we further strengthen assumptions on the Hamiltonian. We show in the sequel that it instead suffices to prove uniform boundedness of solutions to a parametrized family of fixed point problems by leveraging the displacement monotonicity of the model data to ultimately conclude existence of a solution via the Leray–Schauder–Schaefer fixed point theorem.

Definition of discrete solution map

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
X_{0}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
distributed according to
\rho_{0}^{N}
, for some weights
\mathcal{A}_{N}
and partition
\mathcal{O}_{N}
of
\Omega
, and let
T>0
be arbitrary. To begin the proof of the main existence result, we introduce a discrete solution map and prove that it has a fixed point, thereby establishing existence as desired. For each
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
, let the map
T^{k,N}
be defined as follows: for each
Z=(Z_{1},Z_{2})\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, let

T^{k,N}[Z]\coloneqq(T_{1}^{k,N}[Z],T_{2}^{k,N}[Z])\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

uniquely satisfy

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}T_{1}^{k,N}[Z](t)&=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\qquad\qquad\text{ in }[0,T],\\
\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}T_{2}^{k,N}[Z](t)&=-D_{x}g\left(Z_{1}(T),(Z_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)+\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{ in }[0,T].\end{aligned}\right.

(6.1)

Since
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
is a vector subspace of
\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, it is clear that
T^{k,N}
maps
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
into itself, and that any fixed point of
T^{k,N}
is a solution of the numerical scheme (
3.7
), whence
\rho^{k,N}(t)\coloneqq(X^{k,N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}
. The following lemma confirms that this solution map is indeed well-defined since functions in
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
are uniquely determined by their values at the temporal node points
\{0,t_{1},t_{2},\cdots,t_{M_{k}-1},T\}
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For each
Z=(Z_{1},Z_{2})\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
,

T^{k,N}[Z]=(T_{1}^{k,N}[Z],T_{2}^{k,N}[Z])\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

satisfies (
6.1
) if and only if

T_{1}^{k,N}[Z](t_{n})=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s

(6.2)

T_{2}^{k,N}[Z](t_{n})=-D_{x}g\left(Z_{1}(T),(Z_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}D_{x}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s

(6.3)

for
n=0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}
.

Proof.

It is clear that if a function
W=(W_{1},W_{2})\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
satisfies the equations

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}W_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\text{ in }[0,T],

(6.4a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}W_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=-D_{x}g\left(Z_{1}(T),(Z_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)+\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\text{ in }[0,T],

(6.4b)

then we immediately have that

W_{1}(t_{n})=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t_{n}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s

(6.5)

W_{2}(t_{n})=-D_{x}g\left(Z_{1}(T),(Z_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)+\int_{t_{n}}^{T}D_{x}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s

(6.6)

for
n=0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}
. This shows the necessity of the images of
T^{k,N}
satisfying the temporal node conditions (
6.2
), (
6.3
). We now show the sufficiency of these temporal node conditions.

Suppose
W=(W_{1},W_{2})\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
satisfies (
6.5
) and (
6.6
) for
n=0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}
. We clearly then have that
W
satisfies the equations (
6.1
) for
t=0,t_{1},t_{2},\cdots,t_{M_{k}-1},T
. We thus seek to show that these equations hold for all
t\in[0,T]\backslash\{0,t_{1},t_{2},\cdots,t_{M_{k}-1},T\}
. Given
t_{*}\in[0,T]\backslash\{0,t_{1},t_{2},\cdots,t_{M_{k}-1},T\}
there exists
m\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
such that
t_{*}\in(t_{m-1},t_{m})=I_{m}
. We have by definition of
\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}W_{1}|_{I_{m}}
and the fact that
W_{1}
is left-continuous

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}W_{1}(t_{*})&=W_{1}(t_{*})+\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(t_{m}-t_{*}\right)\left(W_{1}|_{I_{m-1}}-W_{1}|_{I_{m}}\right)\\
&=W_{1}(t_{m})+\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(t_{m}-t_{*}\right)\left(W_{1}(t_{m-1})-W_{1}(t_{m})\right)\\
&=W_{1}(t_{m})+\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(t_{m}-t_{*}\right)\int_{t_{m}}^{t_{m-1}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(6.7)

where in the final line we used the temporal node conditions (
6.5
), (
6.6
). Since functions in the space
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
are piecewise constant in time with respect to the temporal partition
\mathcal{J}_{k}
, we get

\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}W_{1}(t_{*})=W_{1}(t_{m})+\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(t_{m}-t_{*}\right)(t_{m-1}-t_{m})D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|_{I_{m}}\\
&=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t_{m}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s-\left(t_{m}-t_{*}\right)D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|_{I_{m}}\\
&=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t_{m-1}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s+\left(t_{*}-t_{m-1}\right)D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|_{I_{m}}\\
&\qquad+\int_{t_{m-1}}^{t_{m}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s+\left(t_{m-1}-t_{m}\right)D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|_{I_{m}}\end{split}

(6.8)

Since

\int_{t_{m-1}}^{t_{m}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s=(t_{m}-t_{m-1})D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|_{I_{m}}

and

\left(t_{*}-t_{m-1}\right)D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|_{I_{m}}=\int_{t_{m-1}}^{t_{*}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s,

we conclude that

\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}W_{1}(t_{*})=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t_{*}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s

(6.9)

As
t_{*}\in[0,T]\backslash\{0,t_{1},t_{2},\cdots,t_{M_{k}-1},T\}
was arbitrary, this shows the sufficiency of the temporal node condition for
W_{1}
. By exacty the same argument, we analogously deduce the sufficiency of the temporal node condition for
W_{2}
. In all, we deduce that the temporal node conditions (
6.2
), (
6.3
) are sufficient for constructing a function
W
that satisfies (
6.1
), thereby completing the proof.
∎

Continuity and compactness of of the discrete solution map

We aim to apply the Leray–Schauder–Schaefer fixed point theorem to prove the existence of a fixed point for
T^{k,N}
. To this end, we show in the next result that
T^{k,N}
is a continuous and compact operator. Equip the finite-dimensional vector space
\mathbb{X}_{k}\coloneqq\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
with the norm
