\lVert(Z_{1},Z_{2})\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}^{2}\coloneqq\|Z_{1}\|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))}^{2}+\|Z_{2}\|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))}^{2}\quad\forall Z=(Z_{1},Z_{2})\in\mathbb{X}_{k}.

(6.10)

where

\displaystyle\|Z_{1}\|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))}^{2}\coloneqq\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}Z_{1}|^{2}+|Z_{1}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{1}(0)|^{2}\right]

(6.11a)

\displaystyle\|Z_{2}\|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))}^{2}\coloneqq\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{2}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s

(6.11b)

for
(Z_{1},Z_{2})\in\mathbb{X}_{k}
.

Remark 6.2
.

The finite dimensional normed spaces
(\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))})
,
(\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))})
are Banach, and hence
\mathbb{X}_{k}
, equipped with the norm (
6.10
), is also finite-dimensional and Banach.

Remark 6.3
.

Let us motivate the choice of the above norm for
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
. We note the following
L^{\infty}
-in-time bound for maps in
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
:

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z(t)|^{2}\right]\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}Z|^{2}+|Z|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\left[|Z(0)|^{2}\right]=\|Z\|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))}^{2}\\
\forall Z\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})).

(6.12)

This is proved directly as follows. The integration-by-parts formula (
5.10
) implies that

\mathbb{E}[|V(t_{n})|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[|V(0)|^{2}]+2\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}V\cdot V\right]{\mathrm{d}s},

for
n=0,1,\cdots,M_{k}
. Apply Young’s inequality to deduce the desired bound (
6.12
) for times
t
at the temporal nodes. But the bound therefore holds also for all time since functions in
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
are left-continuous. Therefore, for each
Z\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
we are able to bound the quantity
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z(t)|^{2}\right]
by the squared norm (
6.11a
). This fact will play a role in establishing the continuity of the solution map
T^{k,N}
.

The continuity and compactness of
T^{k,N}
is now proved in the following result.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let the Hamiltonian
H
satisfy the regularity assumptions (
2.2
), (
2.3
). Let the terminal cost
g
satisfy the regularity assumptions (
2.9
), (
2.10
). There exists a constant
C\geq 0
, which depends only on
T
and the Lipschitz constants of
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
, and
D_{x}g
, such that, for each
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lVert T^{k,N}[Z]-T^{k,N}[W]\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}\leq C\lVert Z-W\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}\quad\forall Z,W\in\mathbb{X}_{k}.

(6.13)

Furthermore,
T^{k,N}
is a compact operator.

Proof.

Notice that, for each
n=1,2,\cdots,M_{k}
, and
Z\in\mathbb{X}_{k}
there hold

T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]|_{I_{n}}=T_{1}^{k,N}[Z](t_{n}),\qquad T_{2}^{k,N}[Z]|_{I_{n}}=T_{2}^{k,N}[Z](t_{n-1})

(6.14)

since
T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]
is left-continuous and
T_{2}^{k,N}[Z]
is right-continuous. Consequently, by the triangle inequality and Hölder inequality in time we get

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]|_{I_{n}}-T_{1}^{k,N}[W]|_{I_{n}}\right|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{1}^{k,N}[Z](t_{n})-T_{1}^{k,N}[W](t_{n})\right|^{2}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t_{n}}D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)-D_{p}H\left(W_{1}(s),W_{2}(s),(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t_{n}}|D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)-D_{p}H\left(W_{1}(s),W_{2}(s),(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[t_{n}\int_{0}^{t_{n}}|D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)-D_{p}H\left(W_{1}(s),W_{2}(s),(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]\\
&\leq T\int_{0}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)-D_{p}H\left(W_{1}(s),W_{2}(s),(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)|^{2}\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(6.15)

where in the final line we used Fubini’s theorem and the fact that

t_{n}\in[0,T]
for
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. By using Lipschitz continuity of
D_{p}H
via (
2.3
) and the elementary bound
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 4(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})
for
a,b,c\in\mathbb{R}
, we get

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]|_{I_{n}}-T_{1}^{k,N}[W]|_{I_{n}}\right|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq 4{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)^{2}T\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{1}-W_{1}|^{2}+|Z_{2}-W_{2}|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}((Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P},(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right]\end{split}

(6.16)

for
Z,W\in\mathbb{X}_{k}
and
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Since
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}((Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P},(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{1}(s)-W_{1}(s)|^{2}\right]
for all
s\in[0,T]
, we get

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]|_{I_{n}}-T_{1}^{k,N}[W]|_{I_{n}}\right|^{2}\right]\leq 8{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)^{2}T\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{1}-W_{1}|^{2}+|Z_{2}-W_{2}|^{2}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}s\right]\end{split}

(6.17)

for
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. Hence, integrating in time the piecewise constant in time map
[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]-T_{1}^{k,N}[W]\right|^{2}\right]
and the above bound give

\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]-T_{1}^{k,N}[W]\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s=\tau_{k}\sum_{k=1}^{M_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]|_{I_{n}}-T_{1}^{k,N}[W]|_{I_{n}}\right|^{2}\right]\\
\leq\tau_{k}\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}8{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)^{2}T\lVert Z-W\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}^{2}\\
=8{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)^{2}T^{2}\lVert Z-W\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}^{2}\quad\forall Z,W\in\mathbb{X}_{k}

(6.18)

since
\tau_{k}M_{k}=T
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. For the difference in the second component
T_{2}^{k,N}[Z]-T_{2}^{k,N}[W]
we likewise deduce that, for
n=1,2,\cdots,M_{k}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{2}^{k,N}[Z]|_{I_{n}}-T_{2}^{k,N}[W]|_{I_{n}}\right|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{2}^{k,N}[Z](t_{n-1})-T_{2}^{k,N}[W](t_{n-1})\right|^{2}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|-D_{x}g\left(Z_{1}(T),(Z_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)+D_{x}g\left(W_{1}(T),(W_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad+\int_{t_{n-1}}^{T}D_{x}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)-D_{x}H\left(W_{1}(s),W_{2}(s),(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|-D_{x}g\left(Z_{1}(T),(Z_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)+D_{x}g\left(W_{1}(T),(W_{1}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{t_{n-1}}^{T}D_{x}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)-D_{x}H\left(W_{1}(s),W_{2}(s),(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq 4{\rm Lip}(D_{x}g)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{1}(T)-W_{1}(T)|^{2}\right]+16{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)^{2}T\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{1}-W_{1}|^{2}+|Z_{2}-W_{2}|^{2}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}s\right]\\
&\leq\left(4{\rm Lip}(D_{x}g)^{2}+16{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)^{2}T\right)\lVert Z-W\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}^{2}\end{split}

(6.19)

due to the Lipschitz continuity of
D_{x}g
,
D_{x}H
via (
2.10
), (
2.3
) and the
L^{\infty}
-bound (
6.12
). This implies that

\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|T_{2}^{k,N}[Z]-T_{2}^{k,N}[W]\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\leq\left(4{\rm Lip}(D_{x}g)^{2}+16{\rm Lip}(D_{x}H)^{2}T\right)T\lVert Z-W\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}^{2}

(6.20)

for all
Z,W\in\mathbb{X}_{k}
.

Given
Z,W\in\mathbb{X}_{k}
, it is clear from the definition of
T^{k,N}
that

\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}T_{1}^{k,N}[Z](t)=D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(t),Z_{2}(t),(Z_{1}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)

(6.21)

\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}T_{1}^{k,N}[W](t)=D_{p}H\left(W_{1}(t),W_{2}(t),(W_{1}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)

(6.22)

after differentiating (
6.1
) for all
t\in[0,T]\backslash\{0,t_{1},t_{2},\cdots,t_{M_{k}-1},T\}
. From this we find that

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]-\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}T_{1}^{k,N}[W]\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H\left(Z_{1}(s),Z_{2}(s),(Z_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)-D_{p}H\left(W_{1}(s),W_{2}(s),(W_{1}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\leq 8{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)^{2}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{1}-W_{1}|^{2}+|Z_{2}-W_{2}|^{2}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}s\right]\end{split}

(6.23)

where the last line was deduced from previous arguments. Hence,

\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}T_{1}^{k,N}[Z]-\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}T_{1}^{k,N}[W]\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\leq 8{\rm Lip}(D_{p}H)^{2}\lVert Z-W\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}^{2}.

(6.24)

Noting that
T_{1}^{k,N}[Z](0)-T_{1}^{k,N}[W](0)=\xi^{N}-\xi^{N}=0
, the bounds (
6.18
), (
6.20
) and (
6.24
) added together imply the desired continuous dependence bound (
6.13
).

Given a bounded sequence
\{Z_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathbb{X}_{k}
, the continuous dependence bound (
6.13
) implies that

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert T^{k,N}[Z_{j}]-T^{k,N}[0]\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}\leq C\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert Z_{j}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}<\infty.

(6.25)

The triangle inequality consequently implies that the sequence
\{T^{k,N}[Z_{j}]\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathbb{X}_{k}
. But since
\mathbb{X}_{k}
is a finite dimensional Banach space with norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{X}_{k}}
, we deduce that
\{T^{k,N}[Z_{j}]\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is sequentially precompact. Therefore,
T^{k,N}
is indeed a compact operator.
∎

Proof of existence of numerical approximations

We now show that solutions to a particular parametrized class of problems is uniformly bounded in the
\mathbb{X}_{k}
-norm, which will allow us to conclude the existence of a fixed point by the Leray–Schauder–Schaefer fixed point theorem. The class of fixed-point problems consider are defined as follows: given
\beta\in[0,1]
, find
Z_{\beta}^{k,N}=(X_{\beta}^{k,N},Y_{\beta}^{k,N})\in\mathbb{X}_{k}
such that
Z_{\beta}^{k,N}=\beta T^{k,N}Z_{\beta}^{k,N}
, i.e.

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)&=\beta X_{0}^{N}+\beta\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(s),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(s),(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\qquad\qquad\text{ in }[0,T],\\
\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)&=-\beta D_{x}g\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(T),(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\beta\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(s),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(s),(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{ in }[0,T]\end{split}

(6.26)

due to linearity of the interpolation operators
\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}
. We note in particular that

Y_{\beta}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),

for all
\beta\in[0,1]
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.1
. For each and
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\beta\in[0,1]
, let
(X_{\beta}^{k,N},Y_{\beta}^{k,N})
satisfy the scaled Hamiltonian system (
6.26
). There exists
k_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\sup_{\beta\in[0,1]}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.\\
\left.+\text{\emph{ess}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\\
\leq C\left[\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right]

(6.27)

where the constants
C
and
k_{\dagger}
depend only on
,c_{0}
,
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
and
D_{x}g
, and the time horizon
T>0
.

Proof.

It is clear that the bound holds if
\beta=0
, so let us assume that
\beta\in(0,1]
. Since
(X_{\beta}^{k,N},Y_{\beta}^{k,N})\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
satisfies the system (
6.26
) by hypothesis and
(0,0,\delta_{0})
uniqely satisfies the generalized system (
5.1
) with
\lambda=0
,
F,G=0
in
\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
and any fixed
X_{0}^{N}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
(see Remark
5.1
), in Theorem
5.1
we take
M=N
,
\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N}=X_{\beta}^{k,N}
,
\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N}=Y_{\beta}^{k,N}
with
\varrho_{1}^{k,N}=(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N})_{\#}\mathbb{P}=(X_{\beta}^{k,N})_{\#}\mathbb{P}
, and
\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M}=0
and
\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M}=0
with
\varrho_{2}^{k,M}=\delta_{0}
where
F_{1}=F_{2}=G_{1}=G_{2}=0
in
\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, and
\lambda_{1}=\beta
,
\lambda_{2}=0
. As such, Theorem
5.1
implies

\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert X_{\beta}^{k,N}\rvert^{2}+\lvert Y_{\beta}^{k,N}\rvert^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert X_{0}^{N}\rvert^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\lvert D_{x}g(0,\delta_{0})\rvert^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left\|D_{p}H(0,0,\delta_{0})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}+\left\|D_{x}H(0,0,\delta_{0})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\right)\end{split}

(6.28)

for all
k\geq k_{\dagger}
,
\beta\in(0,1]
, and
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

We now prove the uniform bound on the partial derivatives of the interpolants. For each
N,k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\geq k_{\dagger}
and each
\beta\in(0,1]
, the pair
(X_{\beta}^{k,N},Y_{\beta}^{k,N})
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)

\displaystyle=\beta D_{p}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}\right)\quad\text{in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})

(6.29a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)

\displaystyle=-\beta D_{x}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}\right)\quad\text{in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})

(6.29b)

\displaystyle\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)

\displaystyle=(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}

(6.29c)

\displaystyle X_{\beta}^{k,N}(0)

\displaystyle=\beta X_{0}^{N},\quad Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(T)=-\beta D_{x}g(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(T),\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}(T))

(6.29d)

where (
6.29a
) and (
6.29b
) hold for all
t\in(0,T)\backslash\iota_{k}
, and the distribution is given by (
6.29c
) for all
t\in[0,T]
. From the linear growth properties (
2.4a
),(
2.4b
) for the partial derivatives of
H
w.r.t.
p
and
x
, respectively, for each
\beta\in(0,1]
we find that

\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}\right)|^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\delta_{0})+1

(6.30)

\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}\right)|^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\delta_{0})+1

(6.31)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, where the hidden constants depend only on
D_{p}H
and
D_{x}H
, respectively. Since we clearly have that
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\delta_{0})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]
for all
t\in[0,T]
, we find that

\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}\right)|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H\left(X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t),\rho_{\beta}^{k,N}\right)|^{2}\right]\\
\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\beta}^{k,N}|^{2}+|Y_{\beta}^{k,N}|^{2}\right]+1

(6.32)

and hence the system (
6.29a
),(
6.29b
),(
6.29c
), (
6.29d
) implies further that

\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{\beta}^{k,N}|^{2}+|Y_{\beta}^{k,N}|^{2}\right]+1

(6.33)

for all
t\in[0,T]\backslash\iota_{k}
. The
L^{\infty}
-bound (
6.28
) on
X_{\beta}^{k,N}
and
Y_{\beta}^{k,N}
then yields

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\text{ess}\sup_{0<t<T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y_{\beta}^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\left(\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right).

(6.34)

where
C^{\prime\prime}
is independent of
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta\in(0,1]
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Fixing
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\geq k_{\dagger}
where
k_{\dagger}
is given by Lemma
6.4
, we immediately deduce from (
6.27
) that there exists a constant
M>0
such that the set

\{Z_{\beta}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{X}_{k}:Z_{\beta}^{k,N}=\beta T^{k,N}Z_{\beta}^{k,N},\text{ }\beta\in[0,1]\}

is contained in the open ball in
\mathbb{X}_{k}
centred at the origin and of radius
M
.
In view of Lemma
6.3
which further shows that
T^{k,N}:\mathbb{X}_{k}\to\mathbb{X}_{k}
is a continuous compact operator, we deduce by the Leray–Schauder-Schaefer fixed-point Theorem
6.1
that
T^{k,N}
admits a fixed point, i.e. there exists a solution to the numerical scheme (
3.7
) for each
k\geq k_{\dagger}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
. This proves Theorem
4.1
.
∎

Convergence of the numerical approximations

We aim to show that, in the joint limit as
k\to\infty
and
N\to\infty
, then
(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N})
converges in a suitable norm to the unique solution of (
2.19
).

Convergence analysis of scheme for fixed
N
as
k\to\infty

The main result of this section asserts the convergence of the numerical scheme (
3.7
) to a continuous time Hamiltonian system as the time-step vanishes while the number of samples
N
of the support of
\rho_{0}
is fixed. In this case, the limit continuous time problem is as follows:
find
(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})\in H^{1}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times H^{1}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
which satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} X^{N}(t)&=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\text{in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})\\
Y^{N}(t)&=-D_{x}g\left(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T)\right)+\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\text{in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})\\
\rho^{N}(t)&=(X^{N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\qquad\text{in }\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\end{aligned}\right.

(7.1)

for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Under the hypotheses on the model data, importantly the displacement monotonicity of
H
and
g
, this problem has at most one solution, see
[
69
, Theorem 4.5]
. The main convergence result that we now proceed to establish also serves as an alternative proof of existence by a compactness argument.

Lemma 7.1

(Convergence for fixed
N
)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.1
and let
N\in\mathbb{N}
be given. Let
\{(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N})\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
denote the sequence generated by the discrete Hamiltonian system (
3.7
) where
k_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
is given by Theorem
5.1
. Then, there exists
(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})\in C^{1}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C^{1}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
which uniquely satisfies (
7.1
). Moreover,

\displaystyle X^{k,N}\to X^{N}{\rm\ and\ }Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}{\rm\ in\ }L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),

(7.2a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\to\partial_{t}X^{N}{\rm\ and\ }\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}\to\partial_{t}Y^{N}{\rm\ in\ }L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),

(7.2b)

\displaystyle X^{k,N}(T)\to X^{N}(T){\rm\ in\ }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),\quad Y^{k,N}(0)\to Y^{N}(0){\rm\ in\ }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(7.2c)

\displaystyle\rho^{k,N}\to\rho^{N}=(X^{N})_{\#}\mathbb{P}\quad{\rm\ in\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\quad\rho^{k,N}(t)\to\rho^{N}(t)\quad{\rm\ in\ }\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}){\rm{\ for}\ }t\in\{0,T\},

(7.2d)

as
k\to\infty
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
.

The proof of this result is based on precompactness of solutions to the discrete-time system (
3.7
), which we establish in the next subsection.

7.1.1
Stability and precompactness

The first step to proving Theorem
7.1
concerns establishing uniform stability for the solution of the scheme (
3.7
) and their time interpolants and corresponding time derivatives.

Lemma 7.2
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.1
. There exist constants
C>0
and
k_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|Y^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.\\
\left.+\text{\emph{ess}}\sup_{0<t<T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\\
\leq C\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right).

(7.3)

Proof.

For fixed
N
, we apply the uniform
L^{\infty}({L}^{2})
-bound given by Lemma
6.4
for
\beta=1
, which then implies

\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|Y^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right.\\
\left.+\text{{ess}}\sup_{0<t<T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\\
\leq C\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right)

(7.4)

for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
, where
C>0
is independent of
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
. It follows from this and the definition of the time-interpolation operators
\mathcal{I}_{\pm}^{k}
that the claimed uniform bound holds:

\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(t)|^{2}+|\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C^{\prime}\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right)\quad\forall N\in\mathbb{N}.

(7.5)

∎

Fixing
N\in\mathbb{N}
, the above result allows us to deduce precompactness of
\{(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N})\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
in the space
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.1
and let
N\in\mathbb{N}
be given. The sequence
\{(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N})\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
generated by the numerical scheme (
3.7
) is precompact in the strong topology of
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
. Moreover,
\{(\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N},\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N})\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
is precompact in the strong topology of
C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
.

We prove this result by adapting the proof of
[
77
, Theorem 6.2.8]
.

Proof.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
be fixed. Since the bound in Lemma
7.2
implies uniform boundedness of the sequence
\{(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N})\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, we have the uniform bound

\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\sup_{0<t_{1}<t_{2}<T}\left[\left\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}X^{k,N}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\left\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}Y^{k,N}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\right]\leq C^{\prime\prime}\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right)

(7.6)

where
C^{\prime\prime}>0
is a constant that is independent of
N\in\mathbb{N}
. We recall here that
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
is the standard norm on
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
that is induced by the expectation on
(\Omega,\mathbb{F},\mathbb{P})
:
\|W\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|W|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}
for
W\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Since
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
is a finite dimensional subspace of
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we conclude that the sequences

\left\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}X^{k,N}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}},\quad\left\{\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}Y^{k,N}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\text{are precompact in $\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})$ }

(7.7)

for all
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<T
. For the remainder of the proof, we will show that
\{X^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
is precompact in
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
for each
p\in[1,\infty))
. We omit the proof of precompactness for the sequence
\{Y^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
since it follows by the same argument.

There holds the equicontinuity bound:

\sup_{s\in(0,T)}\int_{0}^{T-s}s^{-1}\|V(t+s)-V(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\mathrm{d}t\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{\pm}^{k}V\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\mathrm{d}t\quad\forall V\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\pm}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})).

(7.8)

This is shown by the direct argument given in
[
77
, Lemma 6.2.9]
, which uses the fact that maps in
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\pm}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
are piecewise constant-in-time and defined everywhere in
[0,T]
through left or right continuity.
We note that the equicontinuity bound (
7.8
) and Lemma
7.2
allow us to apply the argument of
[
77
, Theorem 6.2.8]
to then obtain that

\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\|X^{k,N}(\cdot+s)-X^{k,N}(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(0,T-s,\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))}\leq(2^{1-\frac{1}{p}}T^{\frac{1}{p}}M_{*,X,N})s^{\frac{1}{p}}\quad\forall s\in(0,T),

(7.9)

for each
p\in[1,\infty)
, where the quantity

M_{*,X,N}\coloneqq\sup_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}\text{ess sup}_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\|X^{k,N}(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\right)

(7.10)

is finite by Lemma
7.2
. Since (
7.7
) and (
7.9
) hold, we apply
[
83
, Theorem 1]
to conclude that the sequence
\{X^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
is precompact in
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
.

We finally show that
\{(\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N},\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N})\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
is precompact in the strong topology of space

C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})).

Observe that Lemma
7.2
and the fact that
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
is a finite dimensional subspace of
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
together imply that
for each
t\in(0,T)
, the sequence
\{(\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(t),\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}(t))\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
is bounded and hence precompact in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})\times\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
. Lemma
7.2
further implies that the sequence
\{(\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N},\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N})\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
is uniformly equicontinuous in time with values in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})\times\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
. Thus, precompactness follows from the Arzela-Ascoli theorem, see e.g.
[
83
, Lemma 1]
.
∎

We then establish the following result on convergence along subsequences.

Lemma 7.4
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.1
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that, for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist
X^{N},Y^{N}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
such that

\displaystyle X^{k,N}\to X^{N}{\rm\ and\ }Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}{\rm\ in\ }L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\quad X^{k,N}(T)\to X^{N}(T){\rm\ in\ }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})

(7.11a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\to{X}^{N}{\rm\ and\ }\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}{\rm\ in\ }C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

(7.11b)

as
k\to\infty
, along a subsequence for all
p\in[1,\infty)
, and

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)|^{2}+|Y^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right).

(7.12)

Proof.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
be given. By Lemma
7.3
there exist
{X}^{N},{Y}^{N}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
such that
\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\to{X}^{N}
and
\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}
in
C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
as
k\to\infty
along a subsequence. This fact allows us to pass to the limit in the uniform bound (
7.3
) of Lemma
7.2
to obtain

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)|^{2}+|Y^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{0}^{N}|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\right)

(7.13)

for a constant
C>0
that is independent of
N\in\mathbb{N}
, thereby proving (
7.12
). By applying Lemma
7.3
once more, for each
p\in[1,\infty)
we pass to further subsequences (without change of notation) to find that there exist
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N},\tilde{Y}_{p}^{N}\in L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
(which depend on
p
in general) such that
X^{k,N}\to\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}
and
Y^{k,N}\to\tilde{Y}_{p}^{N}
in
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
as
k\to\infty
. We claim that
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}=X^{N}
and
\tilde{Y}_{p}^{N}=Y^{N}
in
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
, and hence

X^{k,N}\to X^{N}\text{ and }Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}\text{ in }L^{p}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})

(7.14)

as
k\to\infty
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
. As such, the remainder of the proof seeks to establish
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}=X^{N}
and
\tilde{Y}_{p}^{N}=Y^{N}
in
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
.

We prove the claim for the sequence
\{X^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
as the argument for the sequence
\{Y^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
follows analogously. Indeed, the uniform bound (
7.3
) implies that the sequence
\{\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
. We may then adapt the proof of
[
77
, Theorem 6.2.7]
to deduce that the weak derivative
\partial_{t}\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
exists such that
\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}
converges weakly to
\partial_{t}\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}
in
L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
along a further subsequence as
k\to\infty
, together with the fact that
\xi^{N}=\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}(0)=X^{N}(0)
and
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}(T)=X^{N}(T)
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
. We then deduce that
X^{k,N}(T)=\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(T)\to X^{N}(T)
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
as
k\to\infty
, while noting that
X^{k,N}(0)=\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(0)=X_{0}^{N}=X^{N}(0)
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. This, coupled with the fact that
X^{k,N}\to\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}
and
\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\to{X}^{N}
in
L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
as
k\to\infty
, allow us to deduce from integration-by-parts that

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[-{X}^{N}\cdot\partial_{t}\phi\right]{\mathrm{d}s}&=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[-\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\cdot\partial_{t}\phi\right]{\mathrm{d}s}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\cdot\phi\right]{\mathrm{d}s}=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{t}\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}\cdot\phi\right]{\mathrm{d}s}\end{split}

(7.15)

for all test functions
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
. Hence, the weak derivative
\partial_{t}{X}^{N}
exists with
\partial_{t}{X}^{N}=\partial_{t}\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}
in
L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
, which implies that
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}(t)=X^{N}(t)+z_{p}^{N}
in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
, for some function
z_{p}^{N}\in{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
. Since clearly
{X}^{N}+z_{p}^{N}\in C([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
, we have
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}\in C([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
as well, and so
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}(t)=X^{N}(t)+z_{p}^{N}
in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
for all
t\in[0,T]
. But since
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}(0)=X^{N}(0)
in
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we deduce that
z_{p}^{N}=0
in
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
, which shows that
\tilde{X}_{p}^{N}=X^{N}
in
C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
as claimed.
∎

7.1.2
Proof of Lemma
7.1

Proof.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
be given. Lemma
7.4
implies that there exist
X^{N},Y^{N}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
such that
X^{k,N}\to X^{N}
and
Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}
in
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
and
X^{k,N}(T)\to X^{N}(T)
in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
as
k\to\infty
along a subsequence for all
p\in[1,\infty)
. With this, it is straight-forward to show that
\rho^{k,N}=(X^{k,N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\to\rho^{N}=(X^{N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
\rho^{k,N}(T)\to\rho^{N}(T)
in
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
k\to\infty
along a subsequence. Indeed, since

\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{k,N}(t),\rho^{N}(t))\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{k,N}(t)-X^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\forall t\in[0,T],

(7.16)

we conclude from the convergences
X^{k,N}\to X^{N}
in
L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
with
p=2
and
X^{k,N}(T)\to X^{N}(T)
in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
as
k\to\infty
that
\rho^{k,N}\to\rho^{N}
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
\rho^{k,N}(T)\to\rho^{N}(T)
in
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
k\to\infty
along a subsequence. In all, we have shown with Lemma
7.4
that

\displaystyle X^{k,N}\to X^{N}\text{ and }Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}\text{ in }L^{p}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})),\quad X^{k,N}(T)\to X^{N}(T)\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(7.17a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\to{X}^{N}\text{ and }\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}\to Y^{N}\text{ in }C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

(7.17b)

\displaystyle\rho^{k,N}\to\rho^{N}=(X^{N})_{\#}\mathbb{P}\quad\text{in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})),\quad\rho^{k,N}(T)\to\rho^{N}(T)\quad\text{in }\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

(7.17c)

as
k\to\infty
along a subsequence for any
p\in[1,\infty)
.

Using the convergence (
7.17
) and the uniform Lipschitz continuity of
D_{p}H
,
D_{x}H
and
D_{x}g
via (
2.3
),(
2.10
), we deduce that

\displaystyle D_{p}H\left(X^{k,N}(t),Y^{k,N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t)\right)\to D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(t),Y^{N}(t),\rho^{N}(t)\right)\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

(7.18a)

\displaystyle D_{x}H\left(X^{k,N}(t),Y^{k,N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t)\right)\to D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(t),Y^{N}(t),\rho^{N}(t)\right)\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

(7.18b)

\displaystyle D_{x}g\left(X^{k,N}(T),\rho^{k,N}(T)\right)\to D_{x}g\left(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T)\right)\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})

(7.18c)

as
k\to\infty
, along some subsequence. It then follows from Hölder’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem that

\begin{split}&\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N}\right)-D_{p}H\left(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N}\right)\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq T\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H\left(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N}\right)-D_{p}H\left(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s=0\end{split}

(7.19)

\begin{split}&\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N}\right)-D_{x}H\left(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N}\right)\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\leq T\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{x}H\left(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N}\right)-D_{x}H\left(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s=0.\end{split}

(7.20)

We then deduce from the definition of the numerical scheme (
3.7
) and the uniform convergence of the interpolant terms via (
7.17
) that

X^{N}(t)=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}(t)=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})

(7.21)

Y^{N}(t)=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}(t)=-D_{x}g\left(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T)\right)+\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
\text{in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})

(7.22)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, where
\rho^{N}(t):=(X^{N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}
is in
C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
since
X^{N}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
. We also clearly see that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}X^{N}=D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(t),Y^{N}(t),\rho^{N}(t)\right)\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

(7.23a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Y^{N}=-D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(t),Y^{N}(t),\rho^{N}(t)\right)\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

(7.23b)

We note that these time derivatives are continuous in
[0,T]
since
D_{p}H
and
D_{x}H
are continuous while
X^{N},Y^{N}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
and
\rho^{N}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. As such, we have thus shown for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
the existence of a solution
(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})\in C^{1}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C^{1}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
to the continuous time Hamiltonian system (
7.1
).

In addition, we deduce from the numerical scheme (
3.7
) and the convergence (
7.18
) that

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}-\partial_{t}X^{N}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y^{k,N}-\partial_{t}Y^{N}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H\left(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N}\right)-D_{p}H\left(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N}\right)|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{x}H\left(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N}\right)-D_{x}H\left(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N}\right)|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\to 0\text{ as }k\to\infty.\end{split}

(7.24)

Thus, we have strong convergence:
\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X^{k,N}\to\partial_{t}X^{N}
and
\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}Y^{k,N}\to\partial_{t}Y^{N}
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
as
k\to\infty
along a subsequence. But, by Lemma
2.1
, we have that the solution to (
7.1
) is unique. Hence this convergence and (
7.17
) hold for the entire sequence in the limit as
k\to\infty
. This completes the proof.
∎

Uniform in
N
temporal error bound

In this section we prove the following error bound for the convergence (
7.2a
) as
k\to\infty
for fixed
N\in\mathbb{N}
, which shows in particular that this convergence also holds for
p=\infty
uniformly in time.

Lemma 7.5

(Error bound in time uniform in
N
)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.3
. There holds

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|(Y^{N}-Y^{k,N})(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|(X^{N}-X^{k,N})(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\right)+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))\lesssim\tau_{k}

(7.25)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large and all
N\in\mathbb{N}
, where the hidden constant in (
7.25
) is independent of
k
and
N
.

The analysis will be based on the following projection operators. Let
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}:C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\to\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
,
\Pi_{-}^{k,N}:C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\to\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
be the operators given by

\Pi_{+}^{k,N}\mathscr{X}(t)\coloneqq\mathscr{X}(t_{n})\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),\quad t\in(t_{n-1},t_{n}],\quad n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}

(7.26)

with
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}\mathscr{X}(0)\coloneqq\mathscr{X}(0)
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
for all
\mathscr{X}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, and

\Pi_{-}^{k,N}\mathscr{Y}(t)\coloneqq\mathscr{Y}(t_{n-1})\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),\quad t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n}),\quad n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}

(7.27)

with
\Pi_{-}^{k,N}\mathscr{Y}(T)\coloneqq\mathscr{Y}(T)
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
for all
\mathscr{Y}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
. Given
\mathscr{X}\in C([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, we also define
\pi_{\mathscr{X}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(t)\coloneqq(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}\mathscr{X}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}
, which is the law of the projection
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}\mathscr{X}(t)
, for each
t\in[0,T]
.

The following result provides a temporal error in the projections of the solution

(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})\in C^{1}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C^{1}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

to the continuous time system (
7.1
) for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.3
. There holds

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|(Y^{N}-\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N})(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|(X^{N}-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N})(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\right)+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(t))\lesssim\tau_{k}

(7.28)

for all
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
, where the hidden constant in (
7.25
) is independent of
k
and
N
.

Proof.

Fix
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
. Given
t\in(0,T]
, there exists
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
such that
t\in(t_{n-1},t_{n}]
and hence the system (
7.1
) satisfied uniquely by the triple
(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})
gives

\begin{split}&X^{N}(t)-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t)\\
&=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s-X^{N}(t_{n})\\
&=X_{0}^{N}+\int_{0}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s-X_{0}^{N}-\int_{0}^{t_{n}}D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=-\int_{t}^{t_{n}}D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(7.29)

Since
|t-t_{n}|\leq\tau_{k}
, we apply Hölder’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem to obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{t_{n}}|D_{p}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)|\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\right]\\
\leq 2\tau_{k}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{p}H(0,0,\delta_{0})|^{2}+|D_{p}H(0,0,\delta_{0})|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
\lesssim\tau_{k}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}|^{2}+|Y^{N}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\rho^{N},\delta_{0})\right]\mathrm{d}s+\tau_{k}^{2}|D_{p}H(0,0,\delta_{0})|^{2}

(7.30)

where in the final inequality we used Lipschitz continuity of
D_{p}H
via (
2.3
).
Since
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\rho^{N},\delta_{0})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}|^{2}\right]
in
[0,T]
, we get

\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\lesssim\tau_{k}\int_{t}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[2|X^{N}|^{2}+|Y^{N}|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s+\tau_{k}^{2}|D_{p}H(0,0,\delta_{0})|^{2}

(7.31)

Noting the assumption that
X_{0}^{N}\to X_{0}
in
L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
N\to\infty
, we deduce from Lemma
2.1
the strong convergence of
X^{N}\to X
and
Y^{N}\to Y
in
C([0,T],L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))
as
N\to\infty
. This implies that

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)|^{2}+|Y^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty,

(7.32)

and therefore

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\lesssim\tau_{k}(t_{n}-t)+\tau_{k}^{2}\lesssim\tau_{k}^{2}\end{split}

(7.33)

where the hidden constant is independent of
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Since
t\in(0,T]
was arbitrary and
X^{N}(0)-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(0)=X_{0}^{N}-X^{N}(0)=0
, we conclude that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\tau_{k}.

(7.34)

We find that for each
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n})
with
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}

\begin{split}&Y^{N}(t)-\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}(t)\\
&=-D_{x}g\left(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T)\right)+\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s-Y^{N}(t_{{n-1}})\\
&=-D_{x}g\left(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T)\right)+\int_{t}^{T}D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s+D_{x}g\left(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T)\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{t_{n-1}}^{T}D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=-\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t}D_{x}H\left(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(7.35)

We then likewise deduce from the Lipschitz continuity of
D_{x}H
via (
2.3
), the fact that
|t-t_{n-1}|\leq\tau_{k}
for each
t\in[t_{n-1},t_{n})
with
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
, and the uniform bound (
7.32
) that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|Y^{N}(t)-\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\tau_{k}.

(7.36)

Finally, since
\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(t))\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}(t)-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
for all
t\in[0,T]
, we conclude the proof of the bound (
7.28
). ∎

Remark 7.1
.

The projection error bound (
7.28
) indicates a rate of convergence of order one in the time-step
\tau_{k}
. This rate is optimal since the projections are piecewise constant in time and exactly approximate the zero-order terms
X^{N}(0)
,
Y^{N}(T)
in the first-order temporal Taylor expansions of
X^{N},Y^{N}
, respectively, as shown in the above calculations.

In addition, the projections
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}
satisfy a discrete Hamiltonian system with zeroth-order perturbations which are first order in the time-step in the norm of
L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.

Lemma 7.7
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.3
. For each
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
, there hold

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}=D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)+X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\quad\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(7.37a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}=-D_{x}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)+Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\quad\text{ in }\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}),

(7.37b)

for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
with
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(0)=X_{0}^{N}
and
\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}(T)=-D_{x}g\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(T),\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(T)\right)
, where
X_{pert,avg}^{k,N},Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
with

\displaystyle X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}|_{I_{n}}\coloneqq\tau_{k}^{-1}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left(D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s,

(7.38a)

\displaystyle Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}|_{I_{n}}\coloneqq-\tau_{k}^{-1}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left(D_{x}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{x}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s,

(7.38b)

in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
for
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
and

\left\|X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}+\left\|Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\lesssim\tau_{k}

(7.39)

where the hidden constant is independent of
k
and
N
.

Proof.

Given
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
, the definition of
\Pi_{+}^{k,N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}
and the regularity of
X^{N},Y^{N}
given in Theorem
7.1
clearly imply that
X_{pert,avg}^{k,N},Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}
are well-defined as maps in
\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
. To derive the discrete Hamiltonian system (
7.37
), fix
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
. From the definitions of the interpolation operators
\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k},\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}
and projection operators
\Pi_{+}^{k,N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}
we deduce that

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}|_{I_{n}}&=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\llbracket\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}\rrbracket_{n-1}=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t_{n-1})-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(t_{n})\right)\\
&=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(X^{N}(t_{n-1})-X^{N}(t_{n})\right)\end{split}

(7.40)

The system (
7.1
) satisfied uniquely by the triple
(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})
gives

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}|_{I_{n}}=\tau_{k}^{-1}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\tau_{k}^{-1}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left(D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)|_{I_{n}}\\
&=D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)|_{I_{n}}+X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}|_{I_{n}}\end{split}

(7.41)

where
X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}
is defined in (
7.38a
). We likewise obtain

\begin{split}\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}|_{I_{n}}&=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\llbracket\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}\rrbracket_{n}=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}(t_{n-1})-\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}(t_{n})\right)=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\left(Y^{N}(t_{n-1})-Y^{N}(t_{n})\right)\end{split}

(7.42)

and so

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}|_{I_{n}}=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}D_{x}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})\mathrm{d}s\\
&=-\tau_{k}^{-1}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left(D_{x}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{x}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-D_{x}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)|_{I_{n}}\\
&=-D_{x}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)|_{I_{n}}+Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}|_{I_{n}}\end{split}

(7.43)

where
Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}
is defined in (
7.38b
). Since
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
was arbitrary this establishes the discrete Hamiltonian system (
7.37
). The initial and terminal conditions hold as well since the initial and terminal conditions of the system satisfied by
(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})
can be rewritten in terms of the projection operators
\Pi_{-}^{k,N}
,
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}
and
\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}\rho^{N}
. Indeed,
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(0)=X^{N}(0)=X_{0}^{N}
and
\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(T)=X^{N}(T)
, so

\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}(T)=Y^{N}(T)=-D_{x}g(X^{N}(T),(X^{N}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P})\\
=-D_{x}g(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(T),(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(T))_{\#}\mathbb{P})=-D_{x}g(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}(T),\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}\rho^{N}(T)),

(7.44)

as claimed.

We now prove (
7.39
). The fact that
t_{n}-t_{n-1}=\tau_{k}
for
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
implies

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s=\tau_{k}\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right|^{2}\right]\\
&=\tau_{k}\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tau_{k}^{-1}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left(D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\tau_{k}^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left|D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right|\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\right]\\
&\leq\tau_{k}^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[(t_{n}-t_{n-1})\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\left|D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]\end{split}

(7.45)

where we used Hölder’s inequality to obtain the final inequality.
We then use Lipschitz continuity of
D_{p}H
via (
2.3
) and
t_{n}-t_{n-1}=\tau_{k}
for
n\in\{1,2,\cdots,M_{k}\}
to get

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\leq\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|D_{p}H(X^{N},Y^{N},\rho^{N})-D_{p}H\left(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}\right)\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{N}-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}|^{2}+|Y^{N}-\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\rho^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N})\right]\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(7.46)

Then, the uniform
L^{\infty}({L}^{2})
-bound given in Lemma
7.6
implies that

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right|^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\sum_{n=1}^{M_{k}}\int_{t_{n-1}}^{t_{n}}\tau_{k}^{2}\lesssim\tau_{k}^{2}\end{split}

(7.47)

which yields
\left\|X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\lesssim\tau_{k}
, where the hidden constant is independent of
k
and
N
. An analogous argument also establishes that
\left\|Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\lesssim\tau_{k}
where the hidden constant is independent of
k
and
N
, thereby concluding the proof of the lemma.
∎

We are now able to prove Lemma
7.5
.

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

We establish the desired temporal error bound by leveraging the displacement monotonicity of
H
and
g
via the general continuity bound of the discrete Hamiltonian system as shown in Theorem
5.1
. To this end, let
N,k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\geq k_{\dagger}
. The triangle inequality gives

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|(Y^{N}-Y^{k,N})(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|(X^{N}-X^{k,N})(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq A+B

(7.48)

where

A\coloneqq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|(Y^{N}-\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N})(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|(X^{N}-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N})(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(7.49)

B\coloneqq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,N}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,N}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(7.50)

where
\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,N}^{k}\coloneqq X^{k,N}-\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N}
in
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
and let
\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,N}^{k}\coloneqq Y^{k,N}-\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N}
in
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
. We know from Lemma
7.6
that
A\lesssim\tau_{k}
. We therefore seek to prove that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,N}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,N}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\tau_{k}

(7.51)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, where the hidden constant is independent of
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Now, this is a direct application of the continuous dependence Theorem
5.1
. Indeed, let
M=N
,
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=1
,
X_{0,1}^{N}=X_{0,2}^{N}=X_{0}^{N}
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
, and let
F_{1},G_{1}=0
and
F_{2}\coloneqq X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}
,
G_{2}\coloneqq Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}
in
\mathbb{V}_{k}(0,T;\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, where
X_{pert,avg}^{k,N},Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}
are defined as in Lemma
7.7
. Since
(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N})
and
(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N})
are triples that (uniquely) satisfy the discrete Hamiltonian systems (
3.7
) and (
7.37
), respectively, we apply Theorem
5.1
with

(\mathscr{X}_{1}^{k,N},\mathscr{Y}_{1}^{k,N},\varrho_{1}^{k,N})\coloneqq(X^{k,N},Y^{k,N},\rho^{k,N})

and

(\mathscr{X}_{2}^{k,M},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{k,M},\varrho_{2}^{k,M})=(\Pi_{+}^{k,N}X^{N},\Pi_{-}^{k,N}Y^{N},\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}),

to obtain

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\pi_{X^{N}}^{k}{\rho}^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{Y}_{N,N}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta\mathscr{X}_{N,N}^{k}(t)|^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|X_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}+\|Y_{pert,avg}^{k,N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})}\lesssim\tau_{k}\end{split}

(7.52)

where in the last line we invoked the bound (
7.39
) from Lemma
7.7
. This proves (
7.51
), thereby establishing the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3

Theorem
4.3
on strong convergence of the approximations is deduced from the triangle inquality and application of both Lemma
7.5
and Lemma
2.1
. Indeed, we find that for each
N,k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\geq k_{\dagger}
, the triangle inequality gives

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(Y-Y^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(X-X^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\mathfrak{A}+\mathfrak{B}\end{split}

(7.53)

where

\mathfrak{A}\coloneqq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(t),\rho^{N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(Y-Y^{N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(X-X^{N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(7.54)

\mathfrak{B}\coloneqq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(Y^{N}-Y^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(X^{N}-X^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(7.55)

and
(X,Y,\rho)\in C^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))\times C^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))\times C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
denotes the triple which uniquely satisfies the continuous Hamiltonian system (
1.3
) in the sense of (
2.19
), as given by Lemma
2.1
. Lemma
2.1
also gives
\mathfrak{A}\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}
, while Lemma
7.5
implies that
\mathfrak{B}\lesssim\tau_{k}
since
k\geq k_{\dagger}
. Therefore, we get

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(Y-Y^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(X-X^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\tau_{k}\end{split}

(7.56)

for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\geq k_{\dagger}
. Because
X_{0}^{N}\to X_{0}
in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
as
N\to\infty
and
\tau_{k}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
, we conclude the claimed convergence (
4.3
) in the joint limit as
N,k\to\infty
. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Proof of Corollary
2.2

Using the representation formulae (
2.23
) and (
2.24
) we get for each
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|(u(\cdot,X)-u^{N}(\cdot,X^{N}))(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho(s))-L(X^{N}(s),{D_{p}H(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s))},\rho^{N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\left|g(X(T),\rho(T))-g(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega),

where we used Fubini’s Theorem. Furthermore, for each
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\|(u(\cdot,X)-u^{N}(\cdot,X^{N}))(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq Q_{1}(t)+Q_{2}(t)+Q_{3}+Q_{4},

where

\begin{split}&Q_{1}(t)\coloneqq\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho(s))-L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho^{N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s,\\
&Q_{2}(t)\coloneqq\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho^{N}(s))-L(X^{N}(s),{D_{p}H(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s))},\rho^{N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s,\\
&Q_{3}\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\left|g(X(T),\rho(T))-g(X(T),\rho^{N}(T))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega),\\
&Q_{4}\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\left|g(X(T),\rho^{N}(T))-g(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega).\end{split}

(7.57)

For the remainder of the proof we seek to show that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}Q_{i}(t)\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}},\ \ i\in\{1,2\},\ \ {{\rm and}\ \ Q_{i}\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}},\ \ i\in\{3,4\},}

(7.58)

for all sufficiently large
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

Bounding the terms
Q_{1}
,
Q_{3}
:
Bounding the terms
Q_{1}
,
Q_{3}
relies on the assumption that
L
and
g
exhibit local quantitative continuity via (
2.27a
) and (
2.27b
), respectively. Indeed, fix any
R>0
(e.g., set
R=1
for concreteness). We prove the estimate for
Q_{1}
as the proof for
Q_{3}
is analogous and simpler. Note that the error bound (
2.25
) from Lemma
2.1
implies
\rho^{N}\to\rho
in
C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
as
N\to\infty
. Therefore, picking
N_{R}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho(t),\rho^{N}(t))\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(t),\rho^{N}(t))<R,

for all
N>N_{R}
, the local Lipschitz continuity of
L
via (
2.27a
) implies

\left|L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho(s))-L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho^{N}(s))\right|\\
\leq C_{R}\left(1+|X(s)|+|D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))|+\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho(s),\delta_{0})+\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho^{N}(s),\delta_{0})\right)\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))

(7.59)

for all
s\in[0,T]
and a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
, and hence

\begin{split}&\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho(s))-L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho^{N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq C_{R}\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(1+|X(s)|+|D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))|+\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho(s),\delta_{0})+\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho^{N}(s),\delta_{0})\right)\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(1+|X(s)|+|Y(s)|+\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho(s),\delta_{0})+\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho^{N}(s),\delta_{0})\right)\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(7.60)

where the hidden constant arises due to linear growth of
D_{p}H
(via (
2.4a
)) and is independent of
t\in[0,T]
but depends in particular on
R
.
We then obtain

\begin{split}&\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho(s))-L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho^{N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left(1+\|X(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|Y(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(s),\delta_{0})+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(s),\delta_{0})\right)\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))\end{split}

(7.61)

for all
N>N_{R}
. Since
\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left(\|X(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|Y(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)
is finite and
\rho^{N}\to\rho
in
C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
as
N\to\infty
with estimate
\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}
, we deduce that the first supremum of the left above is uniformly bounded with respect to
N
, thus completing the proof that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}Q_{1}(t)\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}
for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
with
N>N_{R}
sufficiently large. Likewise, it holds that
Q_{3}\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}
for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
with
N>\tilde{N}_{R}\geq N_{R}
sufficiently large (where we note that
\tilde{N}_{R}
depends on
g
and
N_{R}
). It now remains to bound the terms
Q_{2}
and
Q_{4}
.

Bounding the terms
Q_{2}
,
Q_{4}
: Due to the regularity property (
2.13
) for
L
, we use the chain rule and the triangle inequality to obtain

\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X(s),{D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))},\rho^{N}(s))-L(X^{N}(s),{D_{p}H(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s))},\rho^{N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\int_{0}^{1}|D_{x}L(rX(s)+(1-r)X^{N}(s),rV_{*}(s)+(1-r)V_{*}^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s))||X(s)-X^{N}(s)|\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\int_{0}^{1}|D_{v}L(rX(s)+(1-r)X^{N}(s),rV_{*}(s)+(1-r)V_{*}^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s))||V_{*}(s)-V_{*}^{N}(s)|\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s,

(7.62)

where
V_{*}(s,\omega)\coloneqq D_{p}H(X(s),Y(s),\rho(s))
and
V_{*}^{N}(s,\omega)\coloneqq D_{p}H(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s))
for all
s\in[0,T]
and a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
. We consequently obtain from the linear growth of
D_{v}L
and
D_{x}L
via (
2.15a
) and (
2.15b
)

Q_{2}(t)\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(|X(s)-X^{N}(s)|^{2}+|V(s)-V_{*}^{N}(s)||X(s)-X^{N}(s)|\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(|X^{N}(s)|+|V_{*}^{N}(s)|+{\mathcal{W}_{1}}(\rho^{N}(s),\delta_{0})+1)|X(s)-X^{N}(s)|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(|V(s)-V_{*}^{N}(s)|^{2}+|X(s)-X^{N}(s)||V(s)-V_{*}^{N}(s)|\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(|X^{N}(s)|+|V_{*}^{N}(s)|+{\mathcal{W}_{1}}(\rho^{N},\delta_{0})+1)|V(s)-V_{*}^{N}(s)|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s.

(7.63)

Due to Lipschitz continuity of
D_{p}H
via (
2.3
) and the linear growth of
D_{p}H
(see (
2.4a
)), we bound each of
|V-V_{*}^{N}|
and
|V_{*}^{N}|
in the above and collect product terms to eventually obtain

Q_{2}(t)\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(|X(s)-X^{N}(s)|^{2}+|Y(s)-Y^{N}(s)||X(s)-X^{N}(s)|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))|X(s)-X^{N}(s)|\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(|Y(s)-Y^{N}(s)|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(|X^{N}(s)|+|Y^{N}(s)|+{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\rho^{N},\delta_{0})+1)(|X(s)-X^{N}(s)|+|Y(s)-Y^{N}(s)|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s)))\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s.

(7.64)

By applying Young’s inequality, followed by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality applied to each of the integrals over
\Omega
above, we get the estimate

Q_{2}(t)\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\left(\|X(s)-X^{N}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\|Y(s)-Y^{N}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{0}^{T}(\|X^{N}(s)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|Y^{N}(s)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\rho^{N}(s),\delta_{0})+1)(\|X(s)-X^{N}(s)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|Y(s)-Y^{N}(s)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}})\mathrm{d}s,

(7.65)

where we used the estimate
\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho(s),\rho^{N}(s))\leq\|X(s)-X^{N}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, for all
s\in[0,T]
, to obtain an upper bound without the term
\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho,\rho^{N})
. We note that the hidden constant in this bound does not depend on
t\in[0,T]
. By a similar argument that uses the Lipschitz continuity of
D_{x}g
via (
2.10
), we also deduce that

Q_{4}\lesssim\|X(T)-X^{N}(T)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+(\|X^{N}(T)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\rho^{N}(T),\delta_{0})+1)\|X(T)-X^{N}(T)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}.

(7.66)

Since
X_{0}^{N}\to X_{0}
in
{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, we apply Lemma
2.1
to deduce the uniform boundedness of the sequences
\{X^{N}\}_{N}
,
\{Y^{N}\}_{N}
in
C([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
and
\{\rho^{N}\}_{N}
in
C([0,T];\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
from the strong convergence estimate (
2.25
), which in turn implies that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}Q_{2}(t)+Q_{4}\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}},

(7.67)

where we note that the hidden constant in this inequality is independent of
t\in[0,T]
. This completes the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4

Let
k_{\dagger}
be as given by Theorem
4.3
. We note that Corollary
2.2
implies that there exists
N_{\dagger}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(u(\cdot,X)-u^{N}(\cdot,X^{N}))(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}
for all
N\geq N_{\dagger}
. By the triangle inequality, we then have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(u(\cdot,X)-u^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(u(\cdot,X)-u^{N}(\cdot,X^{N}))(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(u^{N}(\cdot,X^{N})-u^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
\lesssim\|X_{0}-X_{0}^{N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(u^{N}(\cdot,X^{N})-u^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(7.68)

for all
N\geq N_{\dagger}
and
k\geq k_{\dagger}
. We are then left with bounding the latter term to complete the proof. Using the representation formula (
2.24
) and the definition of
u^{k,N}
via (
3.13
), we can follow the proof of Corollary
2.2
to likewise get

\|(u^{N}(\cdot,X^{N})-u^{k,N})(t)\|_{L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{Q}_{i}(t){+\sum_{i=3}^{4}\tilde{Q}_{i},}
for all
t\in[0,T]
, where

\begin{split}&\tilde{Q}_{1}(t)\coloneqq\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X^{N}(s),V_{*}^{N},\rho^{N}(s))-L(X^{N}(s),V_{*}^{N},\rho^{k,N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s,\\
&\tilde{Q}_{2}(t)\coloneqq\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|L(X^{N}(s),V_{*}^{N},\rho^{k,N}(s))-L(X^{k,N}(s),V_{*}^{k,N},\rho^{k,N}(s))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega)\mathrm{d}s,\\
&\tilde{Q}_{3}\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\left|g(X^{N}(T),\rho^{N}(T))-g(X^{N}(T),\rho^{k,N}(T))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega),\\
&\tilde{Q}_{4}\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\left|g(X^{N}(T),\rho^{k,N}(T))-g(X^{k,N}(T),\rho^{k,N}(T))\right|\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\omega).\end{split}

(7.69)

where
V_{*}^{N}\coloneqq D_{p}H(X^{N}(s),Y^{N}(s),\rho^{N}(s))
and
V_{*}^{k,N}\coloneqq D_{p}H(X^{k,N}(s),Y^{k,N}(s),\rho^{k,N}(s))
for all
s\in[0,T]
and a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.
Now, Lemma
7.5
implies that

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\|(Y^{N}-Y^{k,N})(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|(X^{N}-X^{k,N})(t)\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\right)+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))\right)\lesssim\tau_{k}\to 0

as
k\to\infty
, while Theorem
4.3
and Lemma
2.1
together imply the boundedness of the sequences
\{X^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger},N}
,
\{Y^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger},N}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
, boundedness of the sequences
\{X^{N}\}_{N}
,
\{Y^{N}\}_{N}
in
C([0,T];{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})})
and boundedness of the sequences
\{\rho^{N}\}_{N}
,
\{\rho^{k,N}\}_{k\geq k_{\dagger},N}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. By taking
k_{\dagger}^{*}\geq k_{\dagger}
sufficiently large so that
\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{1}(\rho^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))\leq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(t),\rho^{k,N}(t))<1=:R
for all
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{*}
, we follow the remaining steps of the proof of Corollary
2.2
to deduce that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\tilde{Q}_{1}(t)+\tilde{Q}_{3}\\
\lesssim\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left(1+\|X^{N}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|Y^{N}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(s),\delta_{0})+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{k,N}(s),\delta_{0})\right)\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\rho^{N}(s),\rho^{k,N}(s))\\
\lesssim\tau_{k}

(7.70)

and that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\tilde{Q}_{2}(t)+\tilde{Q}_{4}\lesssim\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left(\|X^{N}-X^{k,N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\|Y^{N}-Y^{k,N}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right)\\
+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left(\|X^{k,N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|Y^{k,N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\rho^{k,N},\delta_{0})+1\right)\\
\times\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left(\|X^{N}-X^{k,N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}+\|Y^{N}-Y^{k,N}\|_{{{L}^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})}}\right)\lesssim\tau_{k}^{2}+\tau_{k}

(7.71)

for all
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{*}
and all
N\in\mathbb{N}
. The desired error bound (
4.4
) then follows for all
k\geq k_{\dagger}^{*}
and
N\geq N_{*}
.
∎

Numerical Experiments

Algorithms for approximating the discrete Hamiltonian system

In this section we propose two algorithms for approximating the general discrete Hamiltonian system (
3.7
) for each fixed
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
. The first approach is a standard Picard iteration performed globally in time and which is formulated in Algorithm
A
for generic time intervals of the form
[a,b]
and arbitrary initial conditions
X_{init}^{k,N}
and terminal time couplings
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})\to\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
. Note that for Algorithm
A
we allow the trivial case where
\mathcal{T}
is a constant map. Given a momentum approximation
\mathcal{Y}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([0,T];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, the deterministic forward McKean–Vlasov equation in Algorithm
A
is approximated by Algorithm
MKV
. When using this first algorithm to approximate the discrete Hamiltonian system (
3.7
) globally in time, we take
a=0
,
b=T
, and
X_{init}^{k,N}\equiv X_{0}^{N}
,
\mathcal{T}[\mathcal{X}]\coloneqq-D_{x}g\left(\mathcal{X},{\mathcal{X}}_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)
,
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
.

Algorithm A

A standard Picard Iteration on
[a,b]
for the discrete Hamiltonian System

k,N\in\mathbb{N}
;
r=0
;
0<tol<1
;
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.
0\leq a<b
;
X_{init}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
;
Y_{init}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
;
E_{1}\geq 1
; and a map
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})\to\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
.

Set
Y_{0}^{k,N}\leftarrow Y_{init}^{k,N}

while

E_{1}>tol

do

Forward McKean–Vlasov equation:
Compute
X_{r}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
such that

\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X_{r}^{k,N}(t)=X_{init}^{k,N}(a)+\int_{a}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X_{r}^{k,N}(s),Y_{r}^{k,N}(s),(X_{r}^{k,N}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{for }t\in[a,b].

Backward momentum equation:
Compute
Y_{r+1}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
such that

\mathcal{I}_{-}^{k}Y_{r+1}^{k,N}(t)=\mathcal{T}[X_{r}^{k,N}(b)]+\int_{t}^{b}D_{x}H\left(X_{r}^{k,N}(s),Y_{r+1}^{k,N}(s),(X_{r}^{k,N}(s))_{\#}\mathbb{P}\right)\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{for }t\in[a,b].

One-step error update:
Compute the one-step error
E_{1}=\|Y_{r+1}^{k,N}-Y_{r}^{k,N}\|_{L^{2}(a,b;L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))}

If
E_{1}>tol
, set
r+1\leftarrow r
and compute the next iteration.

end

while

Algorithm MKV

Picard Iteration for discrete McKean–Vlasov Equation on
[a,b]

k,N\in\mathbb{N}
;
m=0
;
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.
0\leq a<b
;
0<tol<1
;
\chi\in\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
;
X_{init}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
with
X_{init}^{k,N}(0)=\chi
in
\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
;
\mathcal{Y}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
;
E_{1}\geq 1
.

Set
X_{0}^{k,N}\leftarrow X_{init}^{k,N}

Set
\mu_{0}^{k,N}(t)\leftarrow(X_{0}^{k,N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}

\forall t\in[a,b]

while

E_{1}>tol

do

McKean–Vlasov equation:
Compute
X_{m+1}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
such that

\mathcal{I}_{+}^{k}X_{m+1}^{k,N}(t)=\chi+\int_{a}^{t}D_{p}H\left(X_{m+1}^{k,N}(s),\mathcal{Y}(s),\mu_{m}^{k,N}\right)\mathrm{d}s\text{ for }t\in[a,b].

One-step error:
Compute
E_{1}=\|X_{m+1}^{k,N}-X_{m}^{k,N}\|_{L^{2}(a,b;L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))}
(Note:
E_{1}\geq\|\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{m+1}^{k,N},\mu_{m}^{k,N})\|_{L^{2}(a,b)}
)

Update player distribution
Set
\mu_{m+1}^{k,N}(t)\leftarrow(X_{m+1}^{k,N}(t))_{\#}\mathbb{P}

\forall t\in[a,b]

If
E_{1}>tol
, set
m+1\leftarrow m
and compute the next iteration.

end

while

However, it is known that, in general, the Picard iteration in Algorithm
A
for approximating the Hamiltonian system (
3.7
) is not guaranteed to converge for arbitrarily large time horizon
T
. Therefore, we propose a second algorithm that is inspired by
[
34
]
, where Chassagneux–Crisan–Delarue introduced a numerical method for the approximation of a forward–backward system of stochastic differential equations based on patching together the results of local Picard iterations developed on time
subintervals of
[0,T]
with sufficiently small time-step. The algorithm we propose also performs local Picard iterations but within a global-in-time approximation of the deterministic nonlinear forward-backward discrete Hamiltonian system (
3.7
) for arbitrary time horizon
T
and with displacement monotone data.

To formulate the algorithm more compactly, recall the definition of the spaces
\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\pm}([a,b];A)
from Section
3
. We define related the spaces
\{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\pm}([t_{n},t_{n+1}];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\}_{n=0}^{M_{k}-1}
where the set
\{t_{0},t_{1},\cdots,t_{M_{k}}\}\subset[a,b]
denotes the temporal nodes of the time mesh
\mathcal{J}_{k}
. Furthermore, note that for any function
Q\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\pm}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
, its restriction to a given temporal subinterval
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
satisfies

Q|_{[t_{n},t_{n+1}]}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\pm}([t_{n},t_{n+1}];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

for each
n\in\{0,1,2,\cdots,M_{k}-1\}
. For each
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<tol<1
,
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.
0\leq a<b
,
X_{init}^{k,N}\in V_{k}^{+}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
,
Y_{init}^{k,N}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))
,
E_{1}>1
, and given map
\mathcal{T}:\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})\to\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N})
, we let

\Phi^{k,N}[a,b,tol,X_{init}^{k,N},Y_{init}^{k,N},E_{1},\mathcal{T}]=(\Phi_{X},\Phi_{Y})\in\mathbb{V}_{k}^{+}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))\times\mathbb{V}_{k}^{-}([a,b];\mathcal{R}_{d}(\mathcal{A}_{N},\mathcal{O}_{N}))

denote the output of Algorithm
A
, whenever it converges after some iteration count
r_{*}\in\mathbb{N}
within the tolerance
tol
, where
\Phi_{X}\coloneqq X_{r_{*}}^{k,N}
and
\Phi_{Y}\coloneqq Y_{r_{*}}^{k,N}
. We then define in Algorithm
B
a method based on performing Picard iterations on each of the subintervals
\{[t_{n},t_{n+1}]\}_{n=0}^{M_{k}-1}
along downward induction, c.f.
[
34
]
. In this algorithm we also apply Algorithm
MKV
to approximate the associated discrete McKean–Vlasov equations along the iterations. A pictorial depiction of Algorithm
B
is given in Figure
1
. We note that the use of
L^{2}(L^{2})
